Back: Cleaves SNARE proteins; inhibits release of GABA and glycine from Renshaw cells.

Front: Effect of Botulinum toxin?

Back: Cleaves SNARE proteins; inhibits ACh release at the neuromuscular junction (flaccid paralysis).

Front: Primary reservoir for Listeria monocytogenes?

Back: Unpasteurized dairy, cold deli meats, transplacental/vaginal transmission.

Section 2: Clinical Bacteriology (Systemic)

Front: Major virulence factor of S. pyogenes?

Back: M protein (antiphagocytic, mimics myosin).

Front: Criteria for diagnosing Rheumatic Fever?

Back: JONES criteria (Joints, Carditis, Nodules, Erythema marginatum, Sydenham chorea).

Front: Most common cause of subacute bacterial endocarditis?

Back: Viridans group streptococci (e.g., S. sanguinis).

Front: Identification of S. pneumoniae?

Back: Catalase (-), alpha-hemolytic, lancet-shaped diplococci, bile soluble, Optochin sensitive.

Front: Gold standard test for C. difficile diagnosis?

Back: PCR for toxin genes or stool toxin assay.

Front: Characteristic appearance of Actinomyces israelii?

Back: Yellow "sulfur granules" in abscesses/sinus tracts.

Front: Treatment for Nocardia vs. Actinomyces?

Back: SNAP: Sulfa for Nocardia, Actinomyces use Penicillin.

Front: Primary screening test for Tuberculosis?

Back: PPD (Type IV Hypersensitivity) or IGRA (Interferon-gamma release assay).

Front: Classic presentation of Secondary TB?

Back: Fibrocavitary lesions in the upper lobes of the lung.

Front: Most common cause of UTIs?

Back: Escherichia coli (Uropathogenic strains).

Front: Identifying feature of Klebsiella pneumoniae?

Back: Large mucoid capsule; "currant jelly" sputum in alcoholics/diabetics.

Front: Identifying feature of Proteus mirabilis?

Back: Swarming motility on agar; produces urease (struvite stones).

Front: Common source of Salmonella typhi?

Back: Humans only (gallbladder carriage in chronic carriers).

Front: Classic sign of Vibrio vulnificus infection?

Back: Cellulitis/bullous lesions after exposure to seawater or raw oysters (high risk in liver disease).

Front: Triad of Rocky Mountain Spotted Fever?

Back: Headache, fever, rash (starts at wrists/ankles and spreads centrally).

Section 3: Virology

Front: Only DNA virus that is NOT double-stranded?

Back: Parvoviridae (ssDNA).

Front: Only RNA virus that is NOT single-stranded?

Back: Reoviridae (dsRNA, e.g., Rotavirus).

Front: Site of replication for DNA viruses?

Back: Nucleus (Exception: Poxvirus replicates in cytoplasm).

Front: Site of replication for RNA viruses?

Back: Cytoplasm (Exceptions: Influenza and Retroviruses replicate in nucleus).

Front: Tzanck prep findings in HSV/VZV?

Back: Multinucleated giant cells.

Front: Classic triad of EBV Mononucleosis?

Back: Fever, pharyngitis, lymphadenopathy (posterior cervical).

Front: Cancers associated with EBV?

Back: Burkitt lymphoma, Nasopharyngeal carcinoma, Hodgkin lymphoma.

Front: Most common cause of congenital infection?

Back: Cytomegalovirus (CMV).

Front: Mechanism of Antigenic Shift?

Back: Reassortment of viral genome segments (leads to Pandemics).

Front: High-risk HPV strains for cervical cancer?

Back: HPV 16 and 18 (E6 inhibits p53; E7 inhibits Rb).

Front: Typical finding in Negri bodies?

Back: Rabies virus (cytoplasmic inclusions in Purkinje cells/hippocampus).

Front: Diagnostic finding for Measles (Rubeola)?

Back: Koplik spots (white spots on buccal mucosa).

Front: Sequence of events in HIV infection?

Back: gp120 binds CD4 and CCR5/CXCR4; gp41 mediates fusion.

Section 4: Mycology & Parasitology

Front: Form of fungi at body temperature ($37^\circ\text{C}$)?

Back: Yeast. (Mnemonic: Mold in the Cold, Yeast in the Heat).

Front: Geographic location for Histoplasmosis?

Back: Ohio and Mississippi River Valleys (Bird/Bat droppings).

Front: Morphology of Aspergillus fumigatus?

Back: Septate hyphae with branching at acute angles ($45^\circ$).

Front: Finding in Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia?

Back: Ground-glass opacities on CXR; "crushed ping-pong ball" cysts on silver stain.

Front: Treatment for most systemic mycoses?

Back: Amphotericin B (severe) or Itraconazole (mild/local).

Front: Classic triad of Giardia lamblia?

Back: Bloating, flatulence, foul-smelling fatty stools (steatorrhea).

Front: Flask-shaped ulcers in the colon?

Back: Entamoeba histolytica.

Front: Finding in "Malaria" paroxysms?

Back: Cyclic fever, chills, anemia, splenomegaly.

Front: Vector for Trypanosoma cruzi (Chagas)?

Back: Reduviid bug ("kissing bug").

Front: Characteristic of Enterobius vermicularis?

Back: Pinworms; perianal pruritus (Scotch tape test).

Front: Organism causing "Swiss cheese" appearance in the brain?

Back: Taenia solium (Neurocysticercosis).

Section 5: Antimicrobials & Resistance

Front: Mechanism of Beta-lactams?

Back: Bind Penicillin-Binding Proteins (PBPs); inhibit peptidoglycan cross-linking.

Front: Mechanism of Vancomycin?

Back: Binds D-ala-D-ala portion of cell wall precursors.

Front: Side effect of Aminoglycosides?

Back: Nephrotoxicity, Ototoxicity, Teratogen.

Front: Mechanism of Macrolides?

Back: Bind 50S subunit; inhibit translocation.

Front: Major side effect of Fluoroquinolones?

Back: Tendon rupture (Achilles); cartilage damage in children.

Front: Antibiotic associated with Grey Baby Syndrome?

Back: Chloramphenicol.

Front: Resistance mechanism for MRSA?

Back: Altered PBP (PBP2a) encoded by mecA gene.

Section 6: Immunology Basics (Micro-related)

Front: Cells that release Interferon-gamma (IFN-$\gamma$)?

Back: Th1 cells and NK cells.

Front: Function of Secretory IgA?

Back: Prevents attachment of bacteria/viruses to mucous membranes.

Front: Mechanism of Type I Hypersensitivity?

Back: IgE-mediated mast cell degranulation.

Front: MHC class found on all nucleated cells?

Back: MHC I (presents to CD8+ T cells).

Front: Components of the MAC (Membrane Attack Complex)?

Back: C5b, C6, C7, C8, C9.

Front: Deficiency of C1 esterase inhibitor results in?

Back: Hereditary Angioedema (high bradykinin).

Section 7: Higher-Level Synthesis (Bugs & Clinical Findings)

Front: Bug associated with Guillain-Barré Syndrome?

Back: Campylobacter jejuni.

Front: Most common cause of "Atypical" (walking) pneumonia?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Finding in "Waterhouse-Friderichsen Syndrome"?

Back: Adrenal hemorrhage due to Neisseria meningitidis septicemia.

Front: "Rice-water" stools?

Back: Vibrio cholerae.

Front: "Strawberry cervix"?

Back: Trichomonas vaginalis.

Front: Maltese cross appearance in RBCs?

Back: Babesia microti.

Front: Primary cause of Epiglottitis?

Back: Haemophilus influenzae type b.

Front: "Slapped cheek" rash in children?

Back: Parvovirus B19 (Erythema infectiosum).

Front: Associated bug with staghorn calculi?

Back: Proteus mirabilis.

Front: "Wool-sorter’s disease"?

Back: Pulmonary Anthrax (B. anthracis).

Front: Leading cause of neonatal meningitis?

Back: Group B Streptococcus (S. agalactiae).

Front: Culture requirements for Legionella?

Back: Charcoal yeast extract (BCYE) with Iron and Cysteine.

Front: Typical patient for Pseudomonas infection?

Back: Burn victims, Cystic Fibrosis patients, Intravenous drug users (IVDU).

Front: "Hand-foot-mouth" disease virus?

Back: Coxsackievirus A.

Front: Chronic infection with Schistosoma haematobium increases risk of?

Back: Squamous cell carcinoma of the bladder.

Front: Mechanism of Sulfonamides?

Back: Inhibit dihydropteroate synthase (Dihydropteroate synthase).

Front: Difference between naked and enveloped viruses?

Back: Naked viruses are more resistant to heat/detergents; enveloped viruses acquire membrane from host cell.

Front: Definition of Prion?

Back: Infectious protein (PrPsc) that causes misfolding of normal PrPc proteins.

Front: Primary feature of Borrelia burgdorferi (Lyme)?

Back: Erythema migrans (bull’s-eye rash).

Front: Reservoir for Leptospira interrogans?

Back: Animal urine (often in water/soil).

Front: Major side effect of Amphotericin B?

Back: Nephrotoxicity (electrolyte wasting: $K^+$, $Mg^{2+}$).

Front: Primary prevention for H. influenzae type b?

Back: Conjugate vaccine (capsular polysaccharide + protein).

Front: Identification of Enterococcus species?

Back: Growth in 6.5% NaCl and bile.

Front: Complication of E. coli O157:H7?

Back: HUS (Hemolytic Uremic Syndrome).

Front: Mode of transmission for Toxoplasma gondii?

Back: Cysts in meat or oocysts in cat feces; transplacental.

Front: Classic feature of Bartonella henselae?

Back: Cat-scratch disease (regional lymphadenopathy).

Front: Shape of Vibrio bacteria?

Back: Comma-shaped.

Front: Differentiation of Staph and Strep?

Back: Catalase test (Staph is +, Strep is -).

Front: Differentiation of S. aureus from other Staphylococci?

Back: Coagulase test (S. aureus is +, others are -).

Front: Differentiation of S. epidermidis and S. saprophyticus?

Back: Novobiocin (S. epi is sensitive, S. sapro is resistant).

Front: "Fried-egg" appearance of colonies?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Leading cause of traveler's diarrhea?

Back: ETEC (Enterotoxigenic E. coli).

Front: Characteristic of H. pylori?

Back: Curved rod, Urease positive (used for breath test).

Front: Common symptom of Ascaris lumbricoides?

Back: Loeffler syndrome (pulmonary eosinophilia during lung migration).

Front: Mechanism of Acyclovir?

Back: Guanosine analog; phosphorylated by viral thymidine kinase (chain termination).

Front: Drug of choice for Syphilis?

Back: Penicillin G.

Front: Common cause of viral croup?

Back: Parainfluenza virus (Steeple sign on X-ray).

Front: "Spaghetti and meatballs" appearance on KOH prep?

Back: Tinea versicolor (Malassezia furfur).

Front: Classic sign of Gardnerella vaginalis?

Back: Clue cells; fishy odor (Whiff test).

Front: Most common cause of osteomyelitis in sickle cell patients?

Back: Salmonella.

Front: Finding in Francisella tularensis?

Back: Ulceroglandular disease; transmitted by ticks/rabbits.

Front: "Satellite phenomenon" of H. influenzae?

Back: Growth around S. aureus (which provides NAD+).

Front: Major virulence factor of Cryptococcus neoformans?

Back: Thick polysaccharide capsule (India Ink positive).

Front: Best treatment for Giardiasis?

Back: Metronidazole.

Front: Drug used to treat CMV retinitis?

Back: Ganciclovir.

Front: What is the "window period" in HBV infection?

Back: Period where HBsAg and anti-HBs are both negative; Anti-HBc IgM is positive.

Front: Marker of high infectivity in HBV?

Back: HBeAg.

Front: Mechanism of Metronidazole?

Back: Forms toxic free radicals that damage DNA (anaerobes and protozoa).

Front: Primary treatment for MRSA?

Back: Vancomycin, Daptomycin, or Linezolid.

Front: Appearance of E. coli on EMB agar?

Back: Green metallic sheen.

Front: Bacteria that require Cysteine for growth?

Back: The Four Sisters: Francisella, Brucella, Legionella, and Pasteurella.

Front: Bacteria that are "Obligate Aerobes"?

Back: Nagging Pests Must Breathe: Nocardia, Pseudomonas, Mycobacterium tuberculosis, Bacillus anthracis.

Front: Name the "Obligate Anaerobes"?

Back: Anaerobes Can’t Breathe Fresh Air: Clostridium, Bacteroides, Fusobacterium, Actinomyces.

Front: Distinction between E. coli and Enterobacter on MacConkey?

Back: Both are lactose fermenters (pink), but E. coli is indole positive.

Front: Significance of "Sulfur Granules" in a tissue specimen?

Back: Pathognomonic for Actinomyces israelii.

Front: Identification of Bacillus anthracis on agar?

Back: "Medusa head" appearance (non-hemolytic, filamentous colonies).

Section 9: Zoonotic & Vector-Borne Diseases

Front: Transmission of Brucella?

Back: Unpasteurized dairy products or contact with infected livestock (undulant fever).

Front: Cause of "Rabbit Fever" (Tularemia)?

Back: Francisella tularensis.

Front: Primary vector for Borrelia burgdorferi?

Back: Ixodes tick (also carries Babesia and Anaplasma).

Front: Common infection following a dog or cat bite?

Back: Pasteurella multocida (cellulitis within 24 hours).

Front: Organism causing "Q Fever"?

Back: Coxiella burnetii (atypical pneumonia, no rash, negative Weil-Felix).

Front: Presentation of Leptospira interrogans (Weil Disease)?

Back: Jaundice, photophobia, conjunctival suffusion (redness without exudate).

Front: Vector for Leishmaniasis?

Back: Sandfly.

Section 10: Respiratory & Enteric Pathogens

Front: Definition of "Cold Agglutinins"?

Back: IgM antibodies that agglutinate RBCs at low temperatures; seen in Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Mechanism of Vibrio cholerae diarrhea?

Back: Permanent activation of Gs protein $\rightarrow$ $\uparrow$ cAMP $\rightarrow$ secretion of Cl- into gut lumen.

Front: Most common cause of bacterial enterocolitis in the US?

Back: Campylobacter jejuni.

Front: Bug associated with reactive arthritis (Reiter’s Syndrome)?

Back: Shigella, Salmonella, Campylobacter, Chlamydia.

Front: Identifying feature of Corynebacterium diphtheriae?

Back: Gram-positive rods with "Chinese letter" or club-shaped appearance; metachromatic granules.

Section 11: Helminths & Ectoparasites

Front: Cause of "Biliary Tract Disease" and Cholangiocarcinoma in Asia?

Back: Clonorchis sinensis (Chinese liver fluke).

Front: Mechanism of Vitamin B12 deficiency via parasite?

Back: Diphyllobothrium latum (Fish tapeworm) competes for B12 in the intestine.

Front: Parasite associated with Portal Hypertension and Splenomegaly?

Back: Schistosoma mansoni or Schistosoma japonicum (eggs in mesenteric veins).

Front: Classic symptom of Sarcoptes scabiei?

Back: Intense pruritus (worse at night) and serpiginous burrows in finger webs/wrists.

Front: Finding in Strongyloides stercoralis infection?

Back: Rhabditiform larvae in the stool (can cause hyperinfection syndrome in immunocompromised).

Section 12: High-Yield Mycology

Front: Appearance of Coccidioides immitis in tissue?

Back: Spherules filled with endospores (found in Southwestern US/San Joaquin Valley).

Front: Culture method for most fungi?

Back: Sabouraud dextrose agar.

Front: "Captain's Wheel" yeast formation?

Back: Paracoccidioides brasiliensis (Latin America).

Front: Primary risk factor for Mucor or Rhizopus infection?

Back: Diabetic Ketoacidosis (DKA) or neutropenia (fungus invades cribriform plate).

Section 13: Clinical Syndromes & High-Yield Links

Front: Bug associated with "Currant Jelly" sputum?

Back: Klebsiella pneumoniae.

Front: Cause of "Water-house Friderichsen Syndrome"?

Back: Neisseria meningitidis (leads to adrenal insufficiency/hemorrhage).

Front: Most common cause of "Otitis Externa"?

Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa.

Front: Characteristic rash of "Secondary Syphilis"?

Back: Maculopapular rash on palms and soles; condylomata lata.

Front: Finding in "Congenital Syphilis"?

Back: Hutchinson teeth, saddle nose, saber shins, deafness.

Front: "Double zone of hemolysis" on blood agar?

Back: Clostridium perfringens.

Section 14: Antibiotic Mechanisms & Resistance (Advanced)

Front: Mechanism of Vancomycin resistance?

Back: Mutation of D-Ala-D-Ala to D-Ala-D-Lac.

Front: Mechanism of Tetracyclines?

Back: Bind 30S subunit; prevent attachment of aminoacyl-tRNA.

Front: Effect of Rifampin on the liver?

Back: Potent inducer of Cytochrome P450 (interacts with Warfarin, OCPs).

Front: Mechanism of Trimethoprim?

Back: Inhibits dihydrofolate reductase (DHFR).

Front: Which antibiotic causes "Red Man Syndrome"?

Back: Vancomycin (due to rapid histamine release; prevented by slow infusion).

Front: Unique side effect of Linezolid?

Back: Bone marrow suppression (thrombocytopenia) and Serotonin Syndrome.

Section 15: General Virology (DNA & RNA)

Front: Family of Hepatitis C Virus?

Back: Flaviviridae.

Front: Marker of "Immunity" to Hepatitis B?

Back: Anti-HBs antibody (and negative HBsAg).

Front: Virus causing "Hemorrhagic Cystitis"?

Back: Adenovirus (usually types 11 or 21).

Front: Major characteristic of Reovirus (Rotavirus)?

Back: Segmented, double-stranded RNA virus; major cause of infantile diarrhea.

Front: Virus associated with "Progressive Multifocal Leukoencephalopathy" (PML)?

Back: JC Virus (Polyomavirus).

Front: Which RNA virus is "Ambisese"?

Back: Arenavirus (e.g., Lassa fever).

Front: Defining feature of Prion diseases on histology?

Back: Spongiform encephalopathy (vacuolation of neurons).

Section 16: Systemic High-Yields (Mixed)

Front: Bug associated with infective endocarditis in Colorectal Cancer?

Back: Streptococcus bovis (now S. gallolyticus).

Front: Cause of "Lemierre Syndrome"?

Back: Fusobacterium necrophorum (internal jugular vein thrombosis following pharyngitis).

Front: "Fried-egg" appearance of colonies?

Back: Mycoplasma species.

Front: Common cause of food poisoning from "Reheated Rice"?

Back: Bacillus cereus.

Front: Bug associated with "Rose Spots" on the abdomen?

Back: Salmonella typhi.

Front: Morphology of Neisseria?

Back: Gram-negative "kidney-bean" shaped diplococci.

Front: Cause of "Pseudoappendicitis"?

Back: Yersinia enterocolitica.


Front: Patient with "descending paralysis" after eating home-canned food?

Back: Clostridium botulinum (Botulism).

Front: Child with "barking" cough and "steeple sign" on X-ray?

Back: Parainfluenza virus (Croup).

Front: "Thumbprint sign" on lateral neck X-ray?

Back: Epiglottitis (typically Haemophilus influenzae type b).

Front: Patient with "watery diarrhea" after traveling to Mexico; Lab shows "comma-shaped" organisms?

Back: Vibrio cholerae.

Front: "Currant jelly" sputum in an alcoholic patient?

Back: Klebsiella pneumoniae.

Front: High fever and bradycardia (Faget sign) with "rose spots" on abdomen?

Back: Salmonella typhi (Typhoid fever).

Front: "Bulging fontanelle" and stiff neck in a 2-week-old neonate?

Back: Streptococcus agalactiae (Group B Strep), E. coli, or Listeria.

Front: Facial "sulfur granules" draining from a jaw abscess?

Back: Actinomyces israelii.

Front: Splenic rupture following a sore throat and extreme fatigue?

Back: Epstein-Barr Virus (Infectious Mononucleosis).

Section 18: Mycology & Parasitology (Advanced)

Front: "Owl’s eye" intranuclear inclusions in a biopsy?

Back: Cytomegalovirus (CMV).

Front: "Spaghetti and meatballs" appearance on KOH prep?

Back: Malassezia furfur (Tinea versicolor).

Front: Yeast with "narrow-based budding" and a thick capsule?

Back: Cryptococcus neoformans.

Front: "Maltese cross" inside Red Blood Cells?

Back: Babesia microti.

Front: Flask-shaped colonic ulcers?

Back: Entamoeba histolytica.

Front: Peri-anal itching in a child, diagnosed with a "Scotch Tape" test?

Back: Enterobius vermicularis (Pinworm).

Front: Worm infection associated with B12 deficiency?

Back: Diphyllobothrium latum (Fish tapeworm).

Front: Organism causing "Mega-colon" and "Mega-esophagus" in South America?

Back: Trypanosoma cruzi (Chagas disease).

Section 19: Antibiotics & Resistance Mechanisms

Front: Antibiotic that causes "Yellow Teeth" in children?

Back: Tetracyclines.

Front: Mechanism of Methicillin resistance in S. aureus (MRSA)?

Back: Alteration of Penicillin-Binding Protein (PBP 2a) via mecA gene.

Front: Antibiotic associated with "Red Man Syndrome"?

Back: Vancomycin (infusion-related histamine release).

Front: Antibiotic class that can cause Achilles tendon rupture?

Back: Fluoroquinolones (e.g., Ciprofloxacin).

Front: Medication used for "prophylaxis" in someone exposed to Neisseria meningitidis?

Back: Rifampin (or Ciprofloxacin/Ceftriaxone).

Front: Treatment for Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia (PCP)?

Back: Trimethoprim-Sulfamethoxazole (TMP-SMX).

Front: Mechanism of Macrolide resistance?

Back: Methylation of the 23S rRNA binding site.

Section 20: Viral Essentials

Front: Viral family of HIV?

Back: Retroviridae (Lentivirus genus).

Front: Only DNA virus that replicates in the cytoplasm?

Back: Poxvirus (e.g., Smallpox, Molluscum contagiosum).

Front: Only RNA virus that replicates in the nucleus?

Back: Orthomyxovirus (Influenza) and Retroviruses.

Front: Definition of "Antigenic Shift" in Influenza?

Back: Genetic reassortment of segments (causes pandemics).

Front: Definition of "Antigenic Drift" in Influenza?

Back: Random mutation of Hemagglutinin/Neuraminidase (causes epidemics/seasonal flu).

Front: Virus causing "slapped cheek" rash (Erythema Infectiosum)?

Back: Parvovirus B19.

Front: High-risk HPV types for cervical cancer?

Back: HPV 16 and 18.

Front: Viral cause of "Hemorrhagic Cystitis"?

Back: Adenovirus (Types 11 and 21).

Section 21: High-Yield Culture & Staining

Front: Staining method for Mycobacterium tuberculosis?

Back: Ziehl-Neelsen (Acid-Fast) stain.

Front: Medium used to culture Corynebacterium diphtheriae?

Back: Löffler’s medium or Tellurite agar.

Front: Medium used to culture Neisseria species?

Back: Thayer-Martin (VPN) Agar.

Front: Organism that requires "Charcoal Yeast Extract" agar?

Back: Legionella pneumophila.

Front: "Fried-egg" appearance on culture?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Which bug is "Urease Positive"?

Back: Proteus, H. pylori, Ureaplasma, Nocardia, Cryptococcus, S. saprophyticus. (Mnemonic: PUNCH).

Section 22: Essential Zoonotics

Front: Infection from a "Dog or Cat bite"?

Back: Pasteurella multocida.

Front: "Undulant fever" from unpasteurized dairy?

Back: Brucella species.

Front: Infection from skinning rabbits?

Back: Francisella tularensis.

Front: Vector for "Lyme Disease"?

Back: Ixodes tick.

Front: Vector for "Rocky Mountain Spotted Fever"?

Back: Dermacentor tick.

Section 23: Niche Gram-Negative & Respiratory Pathogens

Front: Diagnostic test for Legionella pneumophila?

Back: Urine antigen test (fastest) or culture on BCYE agar.

Front: Virulence factor of H. influenzae type b (Hib)?

Back: Polyribosylribitol phosphate (PRP) capsule.

Front: Why is Pseudomonas aeruginosa common in Cystic Fibrosis?

Back: It forms a mucoid polysaccharide biofilm (alginate) in the lungs.

Front: "Whooping cough" toxin mechanism?

Back: Pertussis toxin: Disables Gi, increasing cAMP (leads to impaired phagocytosis).

Front: Difference between H. influenzae and H. ducreyi?

Back: H. influenzae causes respiratory issues; H. ducreyi causes "painful" chancroid (STD).

Section 24: High-Yield Viral Complications

Front: Late neurological complication of Measles (Rubeola)?

Back: Subacute Sclerosing Panencephalitis (SSPE) occurring years later.

Front: Virus associated with "Nasopharyngeal Carcinoma"?

Back: Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV).

Front: Typical CBC finding in Infectious Mononucleosis?

Back: Atypical lymphocytes (reactive CD8+ T cells) on peripheral smear.

Front: Only RNA virus with a "Double-Stranded" genome?

Back: Reovirus (Rotavirus).

Front: Most common cause of "Bronchiolitis" in infants?

Back: Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV).

Front: Leading cause of "Fatal Sporadic Encephalitis" in the US?

Back: Herpes Simplex Virus-1 (HSV-1) (affects temporal lobes).

Section 25: Clinical "Best Initial" vs. "Most Accurate" Tests

Front: Best initial test for suspected C. difficile?

Back: Stool PCR or Enzyme Immunoassay (EIA) for toxins A and B.

Front: Gold standard test for "Malaria"?

Back: Thick and thin blood smears.

Front: Most sensitive test for "Chlamydia" and "Gonorrhea"?

Back: Nucleic Acid Amplification Test (NAAT).

Front: Diagnostic test for "Pinworm" (Enterobius)?

Back: Cellulose tape (Scotch tape) test to find eggs perianally.

Section 26: High-Yield Parasitology (Blood & Tissue)

Front: Appearance of Plasmodium falciparum on blood smear?

Back: Crescent-shaped (banana-shaped) gametocytes and multiple ring forms.

Front: Intermediate host of Schistosoma species?

Back: Freshwater snails.

Front: Parasite causing "Elephantiasis" (lymphatic filariasis)?

Back: Wuchereria bancrofti.

Front: Treatment for "Neurocysticercosis" (T. solium)?

Back: Albendazole and Praziquantel (+ steroids to reduce inflammation).

Section 27: Immunology & Micro Crossover

Front: Cytokine that stimulates "Isotype Switching" to IgE?

Back: IL-4.

Front: Immune deficiency making patients susceptible to "Catalase Positive" organisms?

Back: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) due to NADPH oxidase deficiency.

Front: "Opsonins" that enhance phagocytosis?

Back: IgG and C3b.

Front: Mechanism of "Type II" Hypersensitivity?

Back: Cytotoxic: Antibodies (IgG/IgM) bind to antigens on cell surfaces (e.g., Rheumatic Fever).

Front: Component of Gram-negative cell walls that triggers "Septic Shock"?

Back: Lipid A (part of Lipopolysaccharide/LPS).

Section 28: Miscellaneous Board Favorites

Front: "Saber shins" and "Hutchinson teeth" are signs of?

Back: Congenital Syphilis.

Front: Bug associated with "Reiter Syndrome" (can't see, can't spit, can't climb a tree)?

Back: Chlamydia trachomatis.

Front: Most common cause of "Subacute Endocarditis" after dental work?

Back: Viridans group Streptococci.

Front: Bacteria that causes "Struvite" (Staghorn) renal stones?

Back: Proteus mirabilis (via Urease production).

Front: "Ecthyma gangrenosum" is pathognomonic for?

Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa sepsis.

Front: "Waterhouse-Friderichsen Syndrome" involves which gland?

Back: Adrenal glands (hemorrhagic necrosis).

Section 29: Pharmacological Targets

Front: Mechanism of "Sulfonamides"?

Back: PABA antimetabolites; inhibit Dihydropteroate synthase.

Front: Mechanism of "Trimethoprim"?

Back: Inhibits Dihydrofolate reductase.

Front: Side effect of "Ethambutol"?

Back: Optic neuritis (red-green color blindness).

Front: Mechanism of "Amphotericin B"?

Back: Binds ergosterol and forms pores in the fungal cell membrane.

Front: Mechanism of "Caspo-fungin"?

Back: Inhibits synthesis of $\beta$-glucan in the fungal cell wall.

Front: Definition of Bacterial "Transformation"?

Back: Uptake of naked DNA from the environment (notably S. pneumoniae, H. influenzae, Neisseria).

Front: Mechanism of Specialized Transduction?

Back: Lysogenic phage packages specific bacterial genes (e.g., Shiga toxin, Diphtheria toxin).

Front: Function of the bacterial "Sex Pilus"?

Back: Mediates conjugation (transfer of plasmids from $F^+$ to $F^-$ cells).

Front: Difference between "Exotoxin" and "Endotoxin"?

Back: Exotoxins are secreted proteins (high toxicity); Endotoxin is Lipid A of the cell wall (triggers cytokines).

Section 31: Specific Gram-Positive Distinctions

Front: Most common cause of "Prosthetic Valve" Endocarditis?

Back: Staphylococcus epidermidis.

Front: Identification of Streptococcus agalactiae (GBS)?

Back: CAMP test positive (enlarges area of hemolysis formed by S. aureus).

Front: Complication of S. pyogenes that follows only pharyngitis?

Back: Rheumatic Fever (PSGN can follow both pharyngitis and impetigo).

Front: Bug associated with "Gas Gangrene" and "Myonecrosis"?

Back: Clostridium perfringens (Alpha toxin/Lecithinase).

Section 32: Enteric & Gram-Negative Nuances

Front: Distinction between Salmonella and Shigella?

Back: Salmonella is motile and produces $H_2S$ (black on Hektoen); Shigella is non-motile and no $H_2S$.

Front: Main virulence factor of E. coli causing neonatal meningitis?

Back: K1 capsular antigen.

Front: "Comma-shaped" rod that grows at $42^\circ\text{C}$?

Back: Campylobacter jejuni.

Front: Mechanism of Pseudomonas Exotoxin A?

Back: Inactivates Elongation Factor-2 (EF-2) (same mechanism as Diphtheria toxin).

Front: Bug causing "Cat Scratch Disease" vs. "Bacillary Angiomatosis"?

Back: Both caused by Bartonella henselae (Angiomatosis occurs in immunocompromised).

Section 33: Specialized Virology (Hepatitis & HIV)

Front: Which Hepatitis virus requires HBV for coinfection?

Back: Hepatitis D (Deltavirus).

Front: Mode of transmission for Hepatitis E?

Back: Fecal-oral (Enteric); high mortality in pregnant women.

Front: Key structural protein used to diagnose early HIV infection?

Back: p24 capsid protein.

Front: Which HIV gene encodes for Reverse Transcriptase, Protease, and Integrase?

Back: pol gene.

Front: Target of the "Maraviroc" drug in HIV?

Back: CCR5 co-receptor on the host cell.

Section 34: Systemic Mycology "Look-Alikes"

Front: Fungus with "Broad-based budding" found in the Great Lakes region?

Back: Blastomyces dermatitidis.

Front: "Cigar-shaped" yeast associated with rose bushes/gardening?

Back: Sporothrix schenckii.

Front: Distinguishing feature of Mucor vs. Aspergillus?

Back: Mucor has non-septate hyphae with wide-angle ($90^\circ$) branching.

Front: Soap bubble lesions in the brain (Virchow-Robin spaces)?

Back: Cryptococcus neoformans.

Section 35: High-Yield Parasitology Vignettes

Front: Parasite associated with "Squamous Cell Carcinoma of the Bladder"?

Back: Schistosoma haematobium.

Front: Treatment for Toxoplasma gondii?

Back: Sulfadiazine + Pyrimethamine.

Front: "Anchovy paste" liver abscess?

Back: Entamoeba histolytica.

Front: Periorbital edema and muscle pain after eating "undercooked wild game"?

Back: Trichinella spiralis.

Section 36: Antimicrobial Side Effects (Final Round)

Front: Antibiotic that causes "Disulfiram-like reaction" with alcohol?

Back: Metronidazole (also some Cephalosporins).

Front: Classic side effect of "Dapsone"?

Back: Hemolysis in G6PD deficiency.

Front: Unique side effect of "Rifampin"?

Back: Orange-colored body fluids (tears, urine, sweat).

Front: Side effect of "Tetracyclines" involving the skin?

Back: Photosensitivity.

Front: Which antibiotic class causes "Fanconi Syndrome" if expired?

Back: Tetracyclines.

Section 37: Laboratory Diagnostics (Rapid Fire)

Front: "Quellung Reaction" positive?

Back: S. pneumoniae, H. influenzae, N. meningitidis, Klebsiella (Encapsulated bugs).

Front: "Nagler Reaction" identifies?

Back: Clostridium perfringens (Alpha toxin).

Front: "Weil-Felix Reaction" used for?

Back: Rickettsial infections (cross-reacts with Proteus antigens).

Front: "Cold Agglutinin" titer is used for?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae and EBV.

=====================================================================
=====================================================================


Systemic Microbiology:


Front: Major virulence factor of S. aureus that binds the Fc region of IgG?

Back: Protein A.

Front: Most common cause of prosthetic valve endocarditis (subacute)?

Back: Staphylococcus epidermidis.

Front: Differentiation test between Staphylococci and Streptococci?

Back: Catalase test (Staph is +, Strep is -).

Front: Major virulence factor of S. pyogenes that prevents phagocytosis?

Back: M protein.

Front: Criteria used to diagnose Acute Rheumatic Fever?

Back: JONES criteria (Joints, Carditis, Nodules, Erythema marginatum, Sydenham chorea).

Front: Test used to differentiate S. pneumoniae from Viridans strep?

Back: Optochin sensitivity (S. pneumoniae is sensitive).

Front: Common neonatal pathogens transmitted via the birth canal?

Back: S. agalactiae (GBS), E. coli, Listeria.

Bacteriology: Gram-Positive Bacilli

Front: Mechanism of Diphtheria toxin?

Back: Inactivates EF-2 via ADP-ribosylation (inhibits protein synthesis).

Front: Characteristic "Medusa head" morphology on agar?

Back: Bacillus anthracis.

Front: Mechanism of Tetanospasmin?

Back: Cleaves SNARE proteins; inhibits release of GABA and glycine from Renshaw cells.

Front: Only Gram-positive bacteria with endotoxin-like LPS activity?

Back: Listeria monocytogenes.

Front: Treatment for Actinomyces israelii (lumpy jaw)?

Back: Penicillin (SNAP: Sulfa for Nocardia, Actinomyces gets Penicillin).

Bacteriology: Gram-Negative Cocci & Coccobacilli

Front: Growth medium required for N. gonorrhoeae?

Back: Thayer-Martin (VPN) agar.

Front: Serious complication of N. meningitidis involving adrenal hemorrhage?

Back: Waterhouse-Friderichsen syndrome.

Front: Culture requirements for H. influenzae?

Back: Factors V (NAD+) and X (hematin); often grown on Chocolate agar.

Front: Whooping cough stage characterized by repetitive coughing followed by an inspiratory "whoop"?

Back: Paroxysmal stage.

Front: Primary reservoir for Francisella tularensis?

Back: Rabbits/ticks.

Bacteriology: Gram-Negative Bacilli (Enterics & Others)

Front: Leading cause of UTIs and Gram-negative sepsis?

Back: Escherichia coli.

Front: Triad of Hemolytic Uremic Syndrome (HUS)?

Back: Anemia, Thrombocytopenia, Acute Renal Failure.

Front: Common source of Salmonella Typhi in chronic carriers?

Back: Gallbladder.

Front: Complication of Campylobacter jejuni infection?

Back: Guillain-Barré syndrome.

Front: Distinctive "currant jelly" sputum?

Back: Klebsiella pneumoniae.

Front: Mechanism of Vibrio cholerae toxin?

Back: Permanently activates Gs; increases cAMP.

Front: Virulence factor of Pseudomonas that causes ecthyma gangrenosum?

Back: Exotoxin A (same mechanism as Diphtheria toxin).

Front: Triple therapy for H. pylori?

Back: Clarithromycin + Amoxicillin (or Metronidazole) + PPI.

Bacteriology: Zoonotics & Spirochetes

Front: Vector for Borrelia burgdorferi (Lyme disease)?

Back: Ixodes tick.

Front: Stages of Syphilis: Painless chancre?

Back: Primary Syphilis.

Front: Stages of Syphilis: Tabes dorsalis and Gummas?

Back: Tertiary Syphilis.

Front: Classic triad of Rocky Mountain Spotted Fever?

Back: Headache, fever, and rash (starting at wrists/ankles).

Front: Transmission of Coxiella burnetii (Q fever)?

Back: Inhalation of aerosols from cattle/sheep amniotic fluid (no rash).

Mycobacteria & Mycoplasma

Front: Stain used for Mycobacterium tuberculosis?

Back: Ziehl-Neelsen (Acid-fast) stain.

Front: Lowenstein-Jensen medium is used for?

Back: Mycobacterium tuberculosis.

Front: Bacteria lacking a cell wall and containing sterols in the membrane?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Type of anemia associated with Mycoplasma pneumoniae?

Back: Cold agglutinin (IgM) autoimmune hemolytic anemia.

Mycology

Front: Opportunistic fungus with "spaghetti and meatballs" appearance on KOH?

Back: Tinea versicolor (Malassezia furfur).

Front: Endemic location for Histoplasma capsulatum?

Back: Mississippi and Ohio River valleys (bird/bat droppings).

Front: "Captain's wheel" yeast formation?

Back: Paracoccidioidomycosis.

Front: Branching pattern of Aspergillus fumigatus?

Back: Acute angles (< $45^\circ$); septate hyphae.

Front: Branching pattern of Mucor and Rhizopus?

Back: Wide angles ($90^\circ$); non-septate hyphae.

Front: Classic presentation of Cryptococcus neoformans in AIDS patients?

Back: Meningitis (detected by India Ink or Capsular Antigen).

Virology: DNA Viruses

Front: Only DNA virus that is not double-stranded?

Back: Parvovirus (B19).

Front: Site of latency for HSV-1?

Back: Trigeminal nerve ganglia.

Front: Virus associated with Kaposi Sarcoma?

Back: HHV-8.

Front: Receptor used by EBV to enter B cells?

Back: CD21.

Front: Most common cause of sporadic encephalitis in the US?

Back: HSV-1.

Front: Characteristics of Hepatitis B (HBsAg+, HBeAg+, Anti-HBc IgM+)?

Back: Acute HBV infection (high infectivity).

Virology: RNA Viruses

Front: Segmentation of the Influenza virus genome?

Back: 8 segments.

Front: Mechanism of Antigenic Shift?

Back: Reassortment of viral genome segments (leads to pandemics).

Front: Major cause of bronchiolitis in infants?

Back: Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV).

Front: Negri bodies in Purkinje cells are pathognomonic for?

Back: Rabies virus.

Front: Leading cause of non-bacterial gastroenteritis (cruises, schools)?

Back: Norovirus.

Front: Gene in HIV that encodes p24 (capsid protein)?

Back: gag gene.

Front: Gene in HIV that encodes gp120 and gp41?

Back: env gene.

Parasitology & Protozoa

Front: Classic triad of Toxoplasmosis?

Back: Chorioretinitis, hydrocephalus, intracranial calcifications.

Front: "Maltese cross" on blood smear?

Back: Babesia microti.

Front: Drug of choice for Giardia lamblia?

Back: Metronidazole.

Front: Diagnostic stage of Enterobius vermicularis?

Back: Scotch tape test (eggs in perianal folds).

Front: Parasite associated with squamous cell carcinoma of the bladder?

Back: Schistosoma haematobium.

Clinical Syndromes & Pharmacology

Front: Most common cause of Osteomyelitis in Sickle Cell patients?

Back: Salmonella (though S. aureus is still common).

Front: Empiric treatment for neonatal meningitis?

Back: Ampicillin + Gentamicin.

Front: Mechanism of Macrolides (e.g., Azithromycin)?

Back: Bind 50S ribosomal subunit; inhibit translocation.

Front: Major side effect of Aminoglycosides?

Back: Nephrotoxicity and Ototoxicity.

Front: Drug used for prophylaxis in TB contacts?

Back: Isoniazid (INH).

Front: Bacterial cause of "Staghorn calculus" (kidney stones)?

Back: Proteus mirabilis (urease-positive).

Front: Virus associated with subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE)?

Back: Measles virus (Rubeola).

Front: Common cause of "Water-distributor" pneumonia?

Back: Legionella pneumophila.

Front: Diagnostic test for Pinworm?

Back: Cellulose tape test.

Front: Vector for Leishmaniasis?

Back: Sandfly.

Front: Causative agent of "Rose gardener's disease"?

Back: Sporothrix schenckii.

Front: Treatment for MRSA?

Back: Vancomycin, Daptomycin, or Linezolid.

Front: Cause of "Floppy Baby Syndrome" via honey?

Back: Clostridium botulinum spores.

Front: Cause of "Floppy Baby Syndrome" via canned food?

Back: Clostridium botulinum preformed toxin.

Front: Most common cause of Pelvic Inflammatory Disease (PID)?

Back: Chlamydia trachomatis (serotypes D-K) and N. gonorrhoeae.

Front: Characteristic of Pasteurella multocida infection?

Back: Cellulitis following dog or cat bites.

Front: Definition of an "Obligate Intracellular" pathogen?

Back: Cannot make own ATP; must stay inside host cell (e.g., Chlamydia, Rickettsia).

Front: Test used to confirm HIV after a positive ELISA?

Back: Western Blot (though modern protocols use Differentiation Immunoassays).

Front: Major cause of "Swimmer’s Ear"?

Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa.

Front: Organism associated with "Sulfur granules"?

Back: Actinomyces israelii.

Front: Distinguishing feature of Enterococcus species?

Back: Growth in 6.5% NaCl and bile.

Front: Main defense against encapsulated bacteria?

Back: Splenic macrophages (Spleen).

Front: Encapsulated bacteria mnemonic?

Back: "Please SHiNE SKiS" (P. aeruginosa, S. pneumoniae, H. influenzae, N. meningitidis, E. coli, Salmonella, Klebsiella, S. agalactiae).

Front: Mechanism of Vancomycin resistance?

Back: Change of D-Ala-D-Ala to D-Ala-D-Lac.

Front: Complication of Streptococcus pyogenes pharyngitis?

Back: Rheumatic fever or PSGN.

Front: Complication of Streptococcus pyogenes skin infection (Impetigo)?

Back: PSGN only (Rheumatic fever follows pharyngitis only).

Front: Major virulence factor of Cryptococcus neoformans?

Back: Thick polysaccharide capsule.

Front: Drug that causes "Red Man Syndrome"?

Back: Vancomycin.

Front: Vector for Dengue Fever?

Back: Aedes aegypti mosquito.

Front: Bacteria associated with "Whiff test" and Clue cells?

Back: Gardnerella vaginalis.

Front: Treatment for Toxoplasma gondii?

Back: Sulfadiazine + Pyrimethamine.

Front: Unique feature of Treponema pallidum visualization?

Back: Dark-field microscopy.

Front: Mechanism of Beta-lactamase inhibitors?

Back: Protect Penicillins from destruction (e.g., Clavulanic acid, Sulbactam, Tazobactam).

Front: Primary cause of atypical "walking" pneumonia in young adults?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Major symptom of Trichinosis?

Back: Periorbital edema and muscle pain (larvae in muscle).

Front: Virus causing Hydrops Fetalis in utero?

Back: Parvovirus B19.

Front: Viral family of Hepatitis C?

Back: Flavivirus.

Front: Viral family of Hepatitis A?

Back: Picornavirus.

Front: Reservoir for Yersinia pestis (Plague)?

Back: Rats (vector is the flea).

Front: Appearance of E. coli on EMB agar?

Back: Green metallic sheen.

Front: Enzyme that converts Oxygen to Superoxide?

Back: NADPH oxidase (deficient in Chronic Granulomatous Disease).

Front: "Fried-egg" colonies on Eaton agar?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Treatment for anaerobic infections above the diaphragm?

Back: Clindamycin.

Front: Treatment for anaerobic infections below the diaphragm?

Back: Metronidazole.

Front: Most common cause of epiglottitis?

Back: H. influenzae type B (Hib).

Front: Bacteria that utilizes "Actin rockets" for motility?

Back: Listeria monocytogenes and Shigella.

Front: Distinguishing feature of Shigella vs. Salmonella on Hektoen agar?

Back: Salmonella produces $H_2S$ (black colonies); Shigella does not.

Front: Only virus with a double-layered protein capsid?

Back: Reovirus (e.g., Rotavirus).

Front: Virus associated with "Slapped-cheek" rash (Erythema infectiosum)?

Back: Parvovirus B19.

Front: Bacteria causing "Woolsorter’s disease"?

Back: Bacillus anthracis (pulmonary).

Front: "Rice water" stools?

Back: Vibrio cholerae.

Front: "Pearl-like" umbilicated skin papules?

Back: Molluscum contagiosum (Poxvirus).

Front: Mechanism of Sulfonamides?

Back: Inhibit dihydropteroate synthase (folic acid synthesis).

Front: Mechanism of Trimethoprim?

Back: Inhibits dihydrofolate reductase.

Front: Virus that uses Hemagglutinin and Neuraminidase?

Back: Influenza virus.

Front: Vector for Malaria?

Back: Female Anopheles mosquito.

Front: Preferred treatment for Neurosyphilis?

Back: IV Penicillin G.

Front: Most common cause of Traveler’s Diarrhea?

Back: ETEC (Enterotoxigenic E. coli).

Front: Bacteria with bipolar "safety pin" staining?

Back: Yersinia pestis.

Front: Cause of "Fishy" vaginal odor?

Back: Gardnerella vaginalis.

Front: Causative agent of "Brazilian Purpuric Fever"?

Back: H. influenzae biotype aegyptius.

Front: Most common cause of fungal meningitis?

Back: Cryptococcus neoformans.

Front: Toxin that causes "Scalded Skin Syndrome" by cleaving desmoglein-1?Back: Exfoliative toxin (S. aureus).

Front: Mechanism of the Shiga toxin and Shiga-like toxin?Back: Inactivates 60S ribosome by removing adenine from rRNA.

Front: Two bacteria that increase cAMP via Adenylate Cyclase toxin?Back: Bordetella pertussis and Bacillus anthracis (Edema factor).

Front: Enzyme produced by S. pyogenes that degrades DNA in pus to allow spread?Back: DNase B.

Front: Bacteria that produces "Alpha toxin" (lecithinase) leading to gas gangrene?Back: Clostridium perfringens.

Specific Gram-Negative & Respiratory Pathogens

Front: Identification of Legionella via urine?Back: Legionella Urinary Antigen test (specific for serogroup 1).

Front: Characteristic of "Pontiac Fever"?Back: Mild, flu-like form of Legionella infection without pneumonia.

Front: Transmission route for Brucella?Back: Unpasteurized dairy products or contact with infected livestock.

Front: Treatment for Chlamydia (Standard duo)?Back: Ceftriaxone (for Gonorrhea cover) + Doxycycline (for Chlamydia).

Front: Key finding in Lymphogranuloma venereum (LGV)?Back: Painless genital ulcer followed by painful inguinal lymphadenopathy (buboes).

Zoonotics & Rickettsial Diseases

Front: Causative agent of "Cat Scratch Disease"?Back: Bartonella henselae.

Front: Vector for Rickettsia prowazekii (Epidemic typhus)?Back: Human body louse.

Front: "Weil-Felix reaction" is used for?Back: Cross-reacting antibodies to Proteus antigens used to diagnose Rickettsial infections.

Front: Bacteria causing "Ehrlichiosis" (seen in monocytes)?Back: Ehrlichia chaffeensis (transmitted by Amblyomma/Lone Star tick).

Virology: Specialized Concepts

Front: Only RNA virus that replicates in the nucleus?Back: Influenza virus (and Retroviruses).

Front: Virus causing "Councilman bodies" in the liver?Back: Yellow Fever virus.

Front: Most common cause of viral croup (barking cough)?Back: Parainfluenza virus (Type 1).

Front: Mechanism of Acyclovir?Back: Guanosine analog; phosphorylated by viral Thymidine Kinase to inhibit DNA polymerase.

Front: Confirmatory finding for CMV on histology?Back: "Owl’s eye" intranuclear inclusions.

Front: Common cause of Hemorrhagic Cystitis in children?Back: Adenovirus (Serotypes 11 and 21).

Front: Viral cause of Hand-Foot-Mouth disease?Back: Coxsackievirus A16.

Front: Major complication of Poliovirus infection?Back: Destruction of cells in the Anterior Horn of the spinal cord (LMN lesion).

Mycology & Parasitology (Deep Dive)

Front: Classic "Cigar-shaped" yeast?Back: Sporothrix schenckii.

Front: Fungus causing "Fungus Ball" in pre-existing lung cavities?
Back: Aspergillus fumigatus.

Front: Diagnostic finding for Strongyloides stercoralis in stool?Back: Rhabditiform larvae (not eggs).

Front: Parasite causing "Anchovy paste" liver abscess?
Back: Entamoeba histolytica.

Front: Vector for African Sleeping Sickness?
Back: Tsetse fly (Trypanosoma brucei).

Front: Organism causing Chagas disease?Back: Trypanosoma cruzi (vector: Triatomine/Kissing bug).

Front: Major cardiac complication of Chagas disease?
Back: Dilated cardiomyopathy with apical aneurysm.

Front: Leading cause of preventable blindness worldwide?
Back: Chlamydia trachomatis (Serotypes A, B, Ba, C).

Antimicrobials & Resistance

Front: Mechanism of Fluoroquinolones (e.g., Ciprofloxacin)?Back: Inhibit DNA Gyrase (Topoisomerase II) and Topoisomerase IV.

Front: Drug of choice for Rickettsia in pregnant women?
Back: Chloramphenicol (Doxycycline is usually avoided in pregnancy).

Front: Mechanism of Rifampin?
Back: Inhibits DNA-dependent RNA polymerase.

Front: Side effect of Ethambutol?
Back: Optic neuritis (red-green color blindness).

Front: Treatment for Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia (PCP)?Back: TMP-SMX (Bactrim).

Front: Drug that inhibits fungal Ergosterol synthesis by inhibiting Squalene Epoxidase?
Back: Terbinafine.

Front: Drug class that inhibits cell wall synthesis by binding to Ergosterol?
Back: Polyenes (Amphotericin B, Nystatin).

Microbiology Lab/Staining Quick-Fire

Front: "India Ink" is used specifically to visualize?
Back: The capsule of Cryptococcus neoformans.

Front: Bacteria that are "Urease Positive"?
Back: Pee CHUNKS (Proteus, Cryptococcus, H. pylori, Ureaplasma, Nocardia, Klebsiella, S. saprophyticus).

Front: Bacteria that are "Obligate Aerobes"?
Back: "Nagging Pests Must Breathe" (Nocardia, Pseudomonas, Mycobacterium, B. anthracis).

Front: Transformation definition?
Back: Uptake of naked DNA from the environment.

Front: HBeAg signifies?
Back: Active viral replication and high transmissibility.

Front: Anti-HBs signifies?
Back: Immunity (either via recovery or vaccination).

Front: Anti-HBc (Total) with negative HBsAg signifies?
Back: The "Window Period" or a distant resolved infection.

Final High-Yield Systemic Links

Front: Most common cause of "Culture-negative" endocarditis?Back: Coxiella burnetii and HACEK organisms.

Front: Primary infection site for Nocardia?
Back: Lungs (mimics TB; but Gram-positive and weakly acid-fast).

Front: Mechanism of Erythrogenic toxin?
Back: Superantigen; causes Scarlet Fever.

Front: Virus that causes progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy (PML)?Back: JC Virus (Polyomavirus).

Front: Most common cause of myocarditis?
Back: Coxsackievirus B.

Front: Bacteria associated with colon cancer when found in the blood?
Back: Streptococcus gallolyticus (formerly S. bovis).

Front: Most common cause of osteomyelitis in intravenous drug users (IVDUs)?Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa (and S. aureus).

Front: A patient presents with "Bell’s Palsy" and a history of hiking in Connecticut. Diagnosis?
Back: Lyme Disease (Borrelia burgdorferi).

Front: Classic diagnostic finding in the CSF of a patient with HSV-1 encephalitis?
Back: Increased RBCs (hemorrhagic necrosis of temporal lobes).

Front: Bacteria associated with "Double zone of hemolysis" on blood agar?
Back: Clostridium perfringens.

Front: Most common cause of "Spontaneous Bacterial Peritonitis" (SBP)?Back: E. coli (followed by Klebsiella).

Front: Causative agent of "Erysipeloid" (occupational hazard for butchers/fishermen)?Back: Erysipelothrix rhusiopathiae.

Front: Common cause of pneumonia in Alcoholics?
Back: Klebsiella pneumoniae.

Front: Common cause of pneumonia in Cystic Fibrosis patients?
Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa (adults) and S. aureus (children).

Bacterial Genetics & Growth

Front: Specialized transduction is a feature of which five toxins?Back: ABCD'S (Group A Strep toxin, Botulinum toxin, Cholera toxin, Diphtheria toxin, Shiga toxin).

Front: The "Quellung Reaction" (capsular swelling) is positive for which organisms?
Back: Encapsulated organisms (e.g., S. pneumoniae, H. influenzae, N. meningitidis).

Front: Nutrient required by Vibrio vulnificus for growth?
Back: Iron (patients with hemochromatosis are at high risk).

Front: Only bacteria with a polypeptide capsule (containing D-glutamate)?Back: Bacillus anthracis.

Parasitology: Cestodes & Trematodes

Front: Cause of Neurocysticercosis?
Back: Ingestion of Taenia solium eggs (not larvae).

Front: Parasite associated with Vitamin B12 deficiency (Megaloblastic anemia)?
Back: Diphyllobothrium latum (Fish tapeworm).

Front: Characteristic of "Hydatid cysts" in the liver?
Back: Echinococcus granulosus (eggs from dog feces).

Front: Intermediate host for all Trematodes (Flukes)?Back: Freshwater snails.

Front: Parasite associated with Biliary Tract Disease and Cholangiocarcinoma?
Back: Clonorchis sinensis.

Systemic Virology: Tropism & Pathology

Front: Cellular receptor for Parvovirus B19?
Back: Erythrocyte P antigen.

Front: Cellular receptor for Rabies virus?
Back: Nicotinic Acetylcholine Receptor (AChR).

Front: Primary cause of "Aseptic Meningitis"?
Back: Enteroviruses (Coxsackievirus, Echovirus).

Front: Virus that causes "Orchitis" leading to potential sterility in males?Back: Mumps virus.

Front: "Guarnieri bodies" are cytoplasmic inclusions found in?
Back: Poxvirus (Smallpox/Molluscum).

Front: The "Tzanck smear" looks for?
Back: Multinucleated giant cells (indicative of HSV-1, HSV-2, or VZV).

Advanced Antivirals & Antifungals

Front: Mechanism of Oseltamivir (Tamiflu)?
Back: Inhibits Neuraminidase (prevents progeny release).

Front: Mechanism of Griseofulvin?
Back: Interferes with microtubule function; inhibits mitosis.

Front: Main side effect of Ganciclovir?
Back: Bone marrow suppression (leukopenia/thrombocytopenia).

Front: Drug used to treat Chronic Hepatitis C (NS5A inhibitor)?
Back: Ledipasvir (or any drug ending in "-asvir").

Front: Major side effect of Amphotericin B?
Back: Nephrotoxicity ("Amphoterrible").

Summary of High-Yield Labs

Front: Media used for Corynebacterium diphtheriae?
Back: Löffler medium or Tellurite agar.

Front: Media used for Bordetella pertussis?
Back: Bordet-Gengou (potato) agar or Regan-Lowe medium.

Front: Organism identified via "Cold Enrichment"?
Back: Listeria monocytogenes.

Front: Positive "Nagler Reaction" identifies?
Back: Clostridium perfringens (alpha-toxin/lecithinase activity).

Front: "Weil's Disease" triad?
Back: Jaundice, Azotemia (renal failure), and Hemorrhage (from Leptospira interrogans).

Front: Cause of "Malignant Otitis Externa" in Diabetics?
Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa.

Final Potpourri

Front: Common cause of infection following human bites?
Back: Eikenella corrodens.

Front: Mechanism of resistance for Penicillins?
Back: Penicillinase (Beta-lactamase) or altered Penicillin-Binding Proteins (PBP).

Front: Only DNA virus that is NOT icosahedral?
Back: Poxvirus (complex, brick-shaped).

Front: Difference between N. meningitidis and N. gonorrhoeae sugar fermentation?
Back: Meningitidis ferments Maltose and Glucose; Gonorrhoeae only ferments Glucose.

Front: Bacteria that causes "Water-borne" outbreaks in daycare centers?
Back: Giardia lamblia (protozoa) or Shigella.

Front: Leading cause of neonatal sepsis?
Back: Streptococcus agalactiae (Group B Strep).

Front: Most common cause of Myocarditis in the US?
Back: Coxsackie B virus.

Front: Bacteria that produces a toxin that "locks" the jaw?
Back: Clostridium tetani.

Front: Patients with Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) are susceptible to which type of organisms?

Back: Catalase-positive organisms (e.g., S. aureus, Aspergillus, Serratia).

Front: Primary immune deficiency associated with recurrent Neisseria infections?

Back: Terminal Complement Deficiency (C5–C9).

Front: Type of immunity required to clear Listeria or Mycobacterium?

Back: Cell-mediated immunity (T-cells/Macrophages), as they are intracellular.

Front: Mechanism of Superantigens (e.g., TSST-1)?

Back: Cross-link the Beta-region of T-cell receptor to MHC Class II; causes massive cytokine storm (IL-1, IL-2, IFN-$\gamma$, TNF-$\alpha$).

Systemic Mycology: Opportunistic & Rare

Front: Fungus with "Broad-based budding" found in the Great Lakes region?

Back: Blastomyces dermatitidis.

Front: Culture medium specifically for fungi?

Back: Sabouraud dextrose agar.

Front: Most common cause of "fungal" pneumonia in AIDS patients with CD4 < 200?

Back: Pneumocystis jirovecii.

Front: Visualizing Pneumocystis jirovecii requires which stain?

Back: Silver stain (Gomori Methenamine Silver).

Rare Systemic & Tropical Diseases

Front: Causative agent of "Verruga peruana" (skin nodules) and "Oroya fever" (anemia)?

Back: Bartonella bacilliformis.

Front: Vector for Trypanosoma brucei (African Sleeping Sickness)?

Back: Tsetse fly.

Front: "Winterbottom sign" (posterior cervical lymphadenopathy) is seen in?

Back: African Sleeping Sickness (T. brucei).

Front: Mechanism of "Antigenic Variation" in Trypanosoma brucei?

Back: Programmed rearrangement of Variable Surface Glycoproteins (VSG).

Front: Organism causing "Rat-bite fever"?

Back: Streptobacillus moniliformis.

Advanced Bacterial Virulence & Labs

Front: Bacteria that produces a "blue-green" pigment (Pyocyanin)?

Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa.

Front: Bacteria that produces a "red" pigment?

Back: Serratia marcescens.

Front: Bacteria that produces a "yellow" pigment?

Back: Staphylococcus aureus ("Aureus" = gold).

Front: The "Satellite Phenomenon" involves H. influenzae growing around which organism?

Back: Staphylococcus aureus (which provides Factor V/NAD).

Front: "Comma-shaped" bacteria that grows in alkaline media?

Back: Vibrio cholerae.

Front: "S-shaped" or "Seagull-wing" shaped bacteria?

Back: Campylobacter jejuni.

Specific Viral Pathologies

Front: Most common cause of viral "Pink Eye" (Conjunctivitis)?

Back: Adenovirus.

Front: Only RNA virus that is Double-Stranded?

Back: Reovirus (e.g., Rotavirus).

Front: Virus that causes "Aplastic Crisis" in Sickle Cell patients?

Back: Parvovirus B19.

Front: Difference between Variola (Smallpox) and Varicella (Chickenpox) rashes?

Back: Variola: same stage of development. Varicella: multiple stages ("dewdrops on a rose petal").

Front: Leading cause of "Fatal Diarrhea" in children worldwide?

Back: Rotavirus.

Antimicrobials: Resistance & Specific Uses

Front: Drug of choice for Bacteroides fragilis (anaerobe)?

Back: Metronidazole.

Front: Mechanism of Daptomycin?

Back: Disrupts cell membrane of Gram-positive cocci via depolarization.

Front: Why is Daptomycin ineffective for pneumonia?

Back: It is inactivated by pulmonary surfactant.

Front: Mechanism of Linezolid?

Back: Inhibits protein synthesis by binding to the 50S subunit and preventing formation of the 70S initiation complex.

Front: Classic side effect of Linezolid?

Back: Bone marrow suppression (thrombocytopenia) and Serotonin Syndrome.

The "Uniques"

Front: Only bacteria with a "Sterol" containing membrane?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Only bacteria with a "Polypeptide" capsule?

Back: Bacillus anthracis.

Front: Only DNA virus that replicates in the Cytoplasm?

Back: Poxvirus.

Front: Only RNA virus that is "Circular"?

Back: Delta virus (Hepatitis D), Bunya, and Arena viruses.

Final High-Yield Pathology

Front: "Sulfur granules" in draining sinus tracts?

Back: Actinomyces israelii.

Front: "Splinter hemorrhages" and "Janeway lesions" suggest?

Back: Infective Endocarditis (often S. aureus or Viridans strep).

Front: Infection associated with "Reiter Syndrome" (Reactive Arthritis)?

Back: Chlamydia, Shigella, Salmonella, Campylobacter, or Yersinia.

Front: Bacteria associated with "Hot Tub Folliculitis"?

Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa.

Front: Causative agent of "Melioidosis"?

Back: Burkholderia pseudomallei.

Front: Drug used for Leishmaniasis?

Back: Sodium Stibogluconate.

Front: Diagnostic feature of Giardia in stool?

Back: Pear-shaped trophozoites with two nuclei ("Owl’s eyes").

Front: Most common cause of "Brain Abscess" from dental infection?

Back: Viridans streptococci or Fusobacterium.

Front: A patient with HIV has a CD4 count < 50. What is the primary prophylaxis for Mycobacterium avium complex (MAC)?

Back: Azithromycin or Clarithromycin.

Front: Most common cause of "Food Poisoning" from reheated rice?

Back: Bacillus cereus.

Front: "Thumbprint sign" on lateral X-ray of the neck?

Back: Epiglottitis (typically Haemophilus influenzae type B).

Front: "Steeple sign" on AP X-ray of the neck?

Back: Croup (Laryngotracheobronchitis caused by Parainfluenza virus).

Front: Primary infection site for Blastomyces dermatitidis?

Back: Lungs (often disseminates to skin and bone).

Clinical Microbiology: The "Most Commons"

Front: Most common cause of meningitis in adults (all ages combined)?

Back: Streptococcus pneumoniae.

Front: Most common cause of meningitis in teenagers/young adults in dorms?

Back: Neisseria meningitidis.

Front: Most common cause of UTIs in young, sexually active females (second to E. coli)?

Back: Staphylococcus saprophyticus.

Front: Most common cause of fungal endocarditis in IV drug users?

Back: Candida albicans.

Bacterial & Viral Properties (Deep Cut)

Front: Which bacteria contains a "Vi capsular antigen"?

Back: Salmonella Typhi.

Front: Mechanism of the "Toxic Shock Syndrome Toxin-1" (TSST-1)?

Back: Superantigen that binds to MHC II and T-cell receptor (TCR) simultaneously, causing massive IFN-$\gamma$ and IL-2 release.

Front: Identification of Enterococcus species on agar?

Back: Growth in 6.5% NaCl and ability to hydrolyze esculin in the presence of bile (Bile Esculin agar).

Front: Only RNA virus with a "bullet-shaped" envelope?

Back: Rabies virus (Rhabdovirus).

Front: Virus that uses the "CCR5" or "CXCR4" co-receptors for entry?

Back: HIV (gp120 binds to these after binding CD4).

Parasitology & Helminths

Front: "Loeffler syndrome" is associated with the pulmonary phase of which parasite?

Back: Ascaris lumbricoides.

Front: Parasite associated with "Iron Deficiency Anemia" due to blood sucking in the GI tract?

Back: Ancylostoma duodenale or Necator americanus (Hookworms).

Front: Diagnosis of Schistosoma mansoni via stool sample?

Back: Eggs with a prominent "Lateral Spine."

Front: Diagnosis of Schistosoma haematobium via urine sample?

Back: Eggs with a prominent "Terminal Spine."

Front: Treatment for most Trematodes (Flukes) and Cestodes (Tapeworms)?

Back: Praziquantel.

Laboratory Tests & Stains (The Boards Favorite)

Front: "Elek test" is used to detect the toxin of which organism?

Back: Corynebacterium diphtheriae.

Front: "Warthin-Starry stain" (silver stain) is used to visualize which two common pathogens?

Back: Bartonella henselae and Helicobacter pylori.

Front: The "Nagler Test" for C. perfringens detects the activity of?

Back: Alpha-toxin (Lecithinase).

Front: Media used to isolate Vibrio cholerae from stool?

Back: TCBS (Thiosulfate-Citrate-Bile salts-Sucrose) agar.

Front: "Chocolate agar" contains which two specific factors?

Back: Factor X (Hematin) and Factor V (NAD).

Antibiotics: Resistance & Specifics

Front: Resistance to Methicillin (MRSA) is mediated by alterations in?

Back: Penicillin-Binding Proteins (PBP 2a), encoded by the mecA gene.

Front: Mechanism of Vancomycin resistance in Enterococci (VRE)?

Back: Alteration of the D-Ala-D-Ala terminal to D-Ala-D-Lac.

Front: Drug of choice for Lyme Disease in a child < 8 years old?

Back: Amoxicillin (Doxycycline is avoided due to tooth discoloration).

Front: Antibiotic that can cause "Gray Baby Syndrome"?

Back: Chloramphenicol (due to lack of liver UDP-glucuronosyltransferase).

Front: Antibiotic class that can cause "Tendon Rupture" (Achilles)?

Back: Fluoroquinolones.

Clinical Micro "Rules of Thumb"

Front: Infection following a "Human Bite"?

Back: Eikenella corrodens.

Front: Infection following a "Clutch of Chickens" or "Reptiles"?

Back: Non-typhoidal Salmonella.

Front: Infection following "Skinning a Rabbit"?

Back: Francisella tularensis.

Front: Infection following "Exposure to Parrot/Bird Feces"?

Back: Chlamydia psittaci.

Final High-Yield Virology

Front: Virus that causes "Aplastic Crisis" in patients with hereditary spherocytosis?

Back: Parvovirus B19.

Front: Primary cause of "Hemorrhagic Fever" with renal syndrome?

Back: Hantavirus (Bunyavirus).

Front: The only "Live Attenuated" vaccine given to HIV patients (if CD4 > 200)?

Back: MMR and Varicella.

Front: "Koplik spots" are a prodromal sign of?

Back: Measles (Rubeola).

General Hematology: 



Front: Rate-limiting step of Heme synthesis?

Back: $\delta$-aminolevulinic acid (ALA) synthase (requires Vitamin B6).

Front: Location of the erythropoietin (EPO) sensor in the kidney?

Back: Peritubular interstitial cells of the renal cortex.

Front: What state must iron be in to bind Oxygen?

Back: Ferrous state ($Fe^{2+}$). ("It 2nd-ly binds O2").

Front: Major hemoglobin type in adults (HbA1) composition?

Back: $\alpha_2\beta_2$.

Front: Major hemoglobin type in fetuses (HbF) composition?

Back: $\alpha_2\gamma_2$.

Front: Why does HbF have higher oxygen affinity than HbA?

Back: Decreased binding of 2,3-BPG to the $\gamma$-subunits.

Front: Define "Reticulocyte Index" (RI) indicating adequate marrow response to anemia.

Back: $RI > 2\%$.

Section 2: Microcytic Anemias (MCV < 80)

Front: Most common cause of Iron Deficiency Anemia in elderly males?

Back: GI blood loss (occult malignancy until proven otherwise).

Front: Lab findings in Iron Deficiency Anemia (Ferritin, TIBC, Serum Fe)?

Back: $\downarrow$ Ferritin, $\uparrow$ TIBC, $\downarrow$ Serum Iron.

Front: Classic triad of Plummer-Vinson Syndrome?

Back: Iron deficiency anemia, Esophageal webs, Glossitis.

Front: Primary defect in Sideroblastic Anemia?

Back: Protoporphyrin synthesis defect (lead poisoning, Vitamin B6 deficiency, X-linked ALA synthase defect).

Front: Classic biopsy finding in Sideroblastic Anemia (Prussian Blue stain)?

Back: Ringed sideroblasts (iron-laden mitochondria surrounding the nucleus).

Front: Genetic defect in $\alpha$-Thalassemia?

Back: Gene deletion (Chromosome 16).

Front: Genetic defect in $\beta$-Thalassemia?

Back: Point mutation in splice sites/promoter sequences (Chromosome 11).

Front: Hemoglobin electrophoresis finding in $\beta$-Thalassemia Minor?

Back: $\uparrow$ HbA2 ($> 3.5\%$).

Front: Skull X-ray finding in $\beta$-Thalassemia Major?

Back: "Crew-cut" appearance (marrow expansion).

Front: Basophilic stippling is most associated with which Microcytic Anemia?

Back: Lead Poisoning (inhibition of pyrimidine 5'-nucleotidase).

Section 3: Macrocytic Anemias (MCV > 100)

Front: Distinguishing lab finding between B12 and Folate deficiency?

Back: Methylmalonic acid (MMA) is elevated in B12 deficiency ONLY.

Front: Neurological symptom associated with B12 deficiency?

Back: Subacute Combined Degeneration (SCD) of the spinal cord (dorsal columns/lateral corticospinal tract).

Front: Most common cause of Vitamin B12 deficiency?

Back: Pernicious Anemia (anti-intrinsic factor antibodies).

Front: Type of cell seen on peripheral smear in Megaloblastic Anemia?

Back: Hypersegmented neutrophils ($> 5$ lobes).

Front: Common drug causes of Folate deficiency?

Back: Phenytoin, Methotrexate, Trimethoprim.

Section 4: Normocytic Anemias & Hemolysis

Front: Two major categories of Normocytic Anemia based on Reticulocyte Count?

Back: Hemolytic (High RC) vs. Non-hemolytic/Underproduction (Low RC).

Front: Intravascular Hemolysis: Serum Haptoglobin levels?

Back: Decreased (Haptoglobin binds free Hemoglobin).

Front: Confirmatory test for Hereditary Spherocytosis?

Back: Osmotic Fragility Test (or Eosin-5-maleimide flow cytometry).

Front: Mechanism of Paroxysmal Nocturnal Hemoglobinuria (PNH)?

Back: Acquired PIGA gene mutation $\rightarrow$ deficiency of GPI anchor $\rightarrow$ loss of CD55/CD59 (complement inhibitors).

Front: Triad of PNH?

Back: Coombs-negative hemolytic anemia, Pancytopenia, Venous thrombosis (e.g., Budd-Chiari).

Front: Amino acid substitution in Sickle Cell Anemia (HbS)?

Back: Glutamic acid to Valine at position 6 of $\beta$-globin chain.

Front: Amino acid substitution in Hemoglobin C (HbC)?

Back: Glutamic acid to Lysine at position 6 of $\beta$-globin chain. ("LyCine").

Front: Most common trigger for G6PD deficiency oxidative stress?

Back: Fava beans, Infections, Sulfa drugs, Primaquine.

Front: Histologic hallmark of G6PD deficiency?

Back: Heinz bodies and Bite cells.

Front: Warm Agglutinin AIHA: Antibody type and association?

Back: IgG; associated with SLE and CLL.

Front: Cold Agglutinin AIHA: Antibody type and association?

Back: IgM; associated with Mycoplasma pneumoniae and Mononucleosis.

Section 5: White Blood Cell Disorders (Leukemias & Lymphomas)

Front: Translocation associated with Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML)?

Back: $t(9;22)$; BCR-ABL (Philadelphia chromosome).

Front: Marker used to differentiate CML from a Leukemoid Reaction?

Back: Leukocyte Alkaline Phosphatase (LAP). Low in CML, High in Leukemoid Reaction.

Front: Classic marker for Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML)?

Back: Auer Rods (MPO positive).

Front: Specific treatment for AML M3 (Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia)?

Back: All-trans Retinoic Acid (ATRA).

Front: Most common childhood leukemia?

Back: Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL).

Front: Translocation associated with Burkitt Lymphoma?

Back: $t(8;14)$; c-myc activation.

Front: Translocation associated with Follicular Lymphoma?

Back: $t(14;18)$; BCL-2 activation.

Front: Translocation associated with Mantle Cell Lymphoma?

Back: $t(11;14)$; Cyclin D1 activation.

Front: Diagnostic cell for Hodgkin Lymphoma?

Back: Reed-Sternberg cell ("Owl's eye" appearance; CD15+, CD30+).

Front: Clinical hallmark of Multiple Myeloma?

Back: CRAB: Hypercalcemia, Renal insufficiency, Anemia, Bone lytic lesions.

Front: Pathognomonic urine finding in Multiple Myeloma?

Back: Bence-Jones protein (Ig light chains).

Section 6: Hemostasis & Coagulation

Front: Role of von Willebrand Factor (vWF) in primary hemostasis?

Back: Adhesion of platelets to subendothelial collagen via GpIb receptor.

Front: Platelet receptor for Fibrinogen (aggregation)?

Back: GpIIb/IIIa.

Front: Coagulation factor deficiency in Hemophilia A?

Back: Factor VIII.

Front: Coagulation factor deficiency in Hemophilia B?

Back: Factor IX.

Front: Lab profile of DIC (Platelets, PT/PTT, Fibrinogen, D-dimer)?

Back: $\downarrow$ Platelets, $\uparrow$ PT/PTT, $\downarrow$ Fibrinogen, $\uparrow$ D-dimer.

Front: Deficiency in Thrombotic Thrombocytopenic Purpura (TTP)?

Back: ADAMTS13 (vWF cleaving protease).

Front: Pentad of TTP?

Back: Fever, Microangiopathic hemolytic anemia (MAHA), Thrombocytopenia, Renal failure, Neurological symptoms.

Front: Effect of Aspirin on platelet function?

Back: Irreversible inhibition of COX-1 (inhibits $TXA_2$ synthesis).

Front: Mechanism of Heparin?

Back: Activates Antithrombin III.

Front: Antidote for Heparin overdose?

Back: Protamine sulfate.

Front: Mechanism of Warfarin?

Back: Inhibits Epoxide Reductase (interferes with Vitamin K recycling).

Front: Most common inherited hypercoagulable state?

Back: Factor V Leiden (resistance to Protein C cleavage).

Section 7: Myeloproliferative & Miscellaneous

Front: Mutation associated with Polycythemia Vera?

Back: JAK2 kinase mutation.

Front: Clinical symptom unique to Polycythemia Vera?

Back: Aquagenic pruritus (itching after a hot bath).

Front: Major complication of Essential Thrombocythemia?

Back: Bleeding AND thrombosis (paradoxical).

Front: Bone marrow finding in Myelofibrosis?

Back: "Dry tap" (extensive fibrosis) and Teardrop RBCs (Dacrocytes).

Front: Storage form of iron in the marrow?

Back: Hemosiderin.

Front: Target cells are typically seen in which three conditions?

Back: HbC disease, Asplenia, Liver disease, Thalassemia. ("HALT").

Front: Heinz bodies vs Howell-Jolly bodies (Composition)?

Back: Heinz = Denatured Hemoglobin; Howell-Jolly = DNA remnants.

Front: Sideroblastic Anemia vs Lead Poisoning (Enzyme inhibited)?

Back: Sideroblastic = ALA Synthase; Lead = ALA Dehydratase & Ferrochelatase.

Front: Primary site of heme degradation?

Back: Splenic Macrophages (Heme $\rightarrow$ Biliverdin $\rightarrow$ Unconjugated Bilirubin).

Front: Effect of $CO_2$ and $H^+$ (acid) on the Oxygen-Hemoglobin dissociation curve?

Back: Right shift (Bohr effect) $\rightarrow$ increased $O_2$ unloading to tissues.

Section 8: Rapid Review & High-Yield Differentiators

Front: Schistocytes + Normal PT/PTT?

Back: TTP or HUS.

Front: Schistocytes + Prolonged PT/PTT?

Back: DIC.

Front: Isolated $\uparrow$ PTT that corrects with mixing study?

Back: Factor deficiency (e.g., Hemophilia A/B).

Front: Isolated $\uparrow$ PTT that does NOT correct with mixing study?

Back: Coagulation factor inhibitor (e.g., Lupus Anticoagulant).

Front: Massive Splenomegaly + Pancytopenia + TRAP positive?

Back: Hairy Cell Leukemia.

Front: Smudge cells on peripheral smear?

Back: Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL).

Front: Lytic bone lesions + Hypercalcemia + Rash?

Back: Adult T-cell Leukemia/Lymphoma (HTLV-1).

Front: "Starry sky" appearance on lymph node biopsy?

Back: Burkitt Lymphoma.

Front: Birbeck granules (tennis rackets) on EM?

Back: Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis.

Front: Eosinophilic inclusions in the cytoplasm of plasma cells in Multiple Myeloma?

Back: Russell bodies.

Section 9: Advanced RBC & Hemoglobin Pathophysiology

Front: Direct effect of 2,3-BPG on the hemoglobin oxygen affinity?

Back: It stabilizes the T (Taut) state, shifting the curve to the right and promoting $O_2$ unloading.

Front: Why is the ESR (Erythrocyte Sedimentation Rate) increased in inflammation?

Back: Fibrinogen (an acute phase reactant) coats RBCs, neutralizing their negative surface charge (Zeta potential) and allowing them to aggregate (Rouleaux).

Front: Distinction between Hemoglobinuria and Myoglobinuria on dipstick?

Back: Both show "Blood" on dipstick, but only Hemoglobinuria shows pink/red serum (Myoglobin is cleared rapidly, leaving serum clear).

Front: What is the "Rule of Three" in RBC indices?

Back: $RBC \times 3 \approx Hb$ and $Hb \times 3 \approx Hct$.

Front: Most common cause of Death in Sickle Cell patients (Adults)?

Back: Acute Chest Syndrome.

Front: Organism responsible for Osteomyelitis in Sickle Cell patients?

Back: Salmonella (most common/classic) and Staphylococcus aureus.

Front: Effect of Hydroxyurea in Sickle Cell Disease?

Back: Increases production of HbF, which interferes with the polymerization of HbS.

Front: Clinical finding in Lead Poisoning involving the gums?

Back: Burton lines (bluish-gray lead sulfide deposits).

Section 10: Platelet Disorders & Vessel Wall Pathology

Front: Mechanism of Bernard-Soulier Syndrome?

Back: Deficiency of GpIb; impaired platelet-to-vWF adhesion.

Front: Classic finding on peripheral smear for Bernard-Soulier?

Back: Giant platelets (often as large as RBCs).

Front: Mechanism of Glanzmann Thrombasthenia?

Back: Deficiency/defect of GpIIb/IIIa; impaired platelet-to-platelet aggregation.

Front: Ristocetin cofactor assay result in von Willebrand Disease (vWD)?

Back: Abnormal (no agglutination), because vWF is required for Ristocetin-induced platelet clumping.

Front: Why is the PTT sometimes prolonged in vWD?

Back: vWF acts as a stabilizer/carrier for Factor VIII.

Front: What is the "Pseudo-Thrombocytopenia" artifact in lab reports?

Back: Platelet clumping in the test tube caused by EDTA (requires re-testing with a citrate tube).

Front: Mechanism of Immune Thrombocytopenic Purpura (ITP)?

Back: Anti-GpIIb/IIIa antibodies $\rightarrow$ splenic macrophage consumption of platelets.

Front: First-line treatment for ITP in a patient with a dangerously low platelet count?

Back: IVIG (acts as a "decoy" for splenic macrophages) or Corticosteroids.

Section 11: Hypercoagulability & Anticoagulation

Front: Effect of Protein C/S deficiency on the body?

Back: Hypercoagulability; unable to inactivate Factors Va and VIIIa.

Front: Why does Warfarin cause "Skin Necrosis" in Protein C deficient patients?

Back: Protein C has a shorter half-life than Factors II, IX, and X; its rapid depletion creates a transient hypercoagulable state.

Front: Mechanism of Antithrombin III deficiency?

Back: PTT fails to rise significantly after giving Heparin (Heparin resistance).

Front: Difference between UFH (Unfractionated Heparin) and LMWH (Enoxaparin) mechanism?

Back: UFH inhibits both Thrombin and Factor Xa; LMWH predominantly inhibits Factor Xa.

Front: Lab test used to monitor Warfarin therapy?

Back: PT / INR.

Front: Direct Thrombin Inhibitors (e.g., Argatroban, Bivalirudin) are the treatment of choice for?

Back: Heparin-Induced Thrombocytopenia (HIT).

Section 12: Myeloid & Lymphoid Malignancies (Detailed)

Front: Mutation found in almost 100% of Hairy Cell Leukemia cases?

Back: BRAF V600E.

Front: Distinctive "Dry Tap" on bone marrow aspiration is seen in which two conditions?

Back: Primary Myelofibrosis and Hairy Cell Leukemia.

Front: Difference between Leukemoid Reaction and CML regarding Basophils?

Back: Basophilia is characteristic of CML, not a Leukemoid Reaction.

Front: Typical immunophenotype of CLL B-cells?

Back: CD5+ and CD23+ (Note: CD5 is usually a T-cell marker).

Front: What is a "Richter Transformation"?

Back: CLL/SLL transforming into an aggressive Diffuse Large B-Cell Lymphoma (DLBCL).

Front: "Waldenström Macroglobulinemia" involves the overproduction of which Ig?

Back: IgM (leads to hyperviscosity syndrome).

Front: Radiographic "Punch-out" lesions in Multiple Myeloma are caused by?

Back: RANKL activation of Osteoclasts (and inhibition of Osteoblasts).

Front: Key difference between MGUS and Multiple Myeloma?

Back: MGUS has $< 10\%$ plasma cells in marrow and NO "CRAB" symptoms.

Section 13: Transfusion Medicine & Miscellaneous

Front: Most common transfusion reaction?

Back: Febrile Non-hemolytic Transfusion Reaction (caused by cytokines in stored blood).

Front: Primary symptom of TRALI (Transfusion-Related Acute Lung Injury)?

Back: Non-cardiogenic pulmonary edema (sudden onset respiratory distress/hypoxia).

Front: Mechanism of Acute Hemolytic Transfusion Reaction?

Back: Type II Hypersensitivity (preformed antibodies against donor ABO antigens).

Front: Blood product used to rapidly reverse Warfarin?

Back: Prothrombin Complex Concentrate (PCC) or Fresh Frozen Plasma (FFP).

Front: Indication for giving "Washed" Red Blood Cells?

Back: IgA deficiency (to prevent anaphylaxis).

Front: Storage lesion of RBCs involves a decrease in which metabolite?

Back: 2,3-BPG (leads to increased $O_2$ affinity and decreased tissue delivery).

Front: Definition of Pancytopenia?

Back: Decrease in all three cell lines (RBCs, WBCs, and Platelets).

Front: Most common cause of Aplastic Anemia?

Back: Idiopathic (immune-mediated T-cell destruction of stem cells).

Front: Morphology of RBCs in Myelophthisic Anemia?

Back: Teardrop cells (Dacrocytes) – caused by the marrow being "squeezed" by fibrosis or tumor.

Section 14: Storage & Metabolic Disorders (Heme-related)

Front: Deficiency in Acute Intermittent Porphyria (AIP)?

Back: Porphobilinogen (PBG) deaminase.

Front: The "5 Ps" of Acute Intermittent Porphyria symptoms?

Back: Painful abdomen, Port wine-colored urine, Polyneuropathy, Psychological disturbances, Precipitated by drugs (CYP450 inducers).

Front: Deficiency in Porphyria Cutanea Tarda (PCT)?

Back: Uroporphyrinogen decarboxylase.

Front: Classic clinical presentation of Porphyria Cutanea Tarda?

Back: Blistering cutaneous photosensitivity and hyperpigmentation (often associated with Hepatitis C).

Front: Iron overload in the heart (Hemochromatosis) typically leads to?

Back: Restrictive or Dilated Cardiomyopathy.

Section 15: Laboratory Medicine & Flow Cytometry

Front: Marker for identifying Hematopoietic Stem Cells (HSC)?

Back: CD34+.

Front: Markers used to identify B-cell lineage via Flow Cytometry?

Back: CD19, CD20, and CD21.

Front: Marker used to identify the monocyte lineage?

Back: CD14 (the receptor for LPS).

Front: What does a "Shift to the Left" signify in a CBC?

Back: Increased presence of immature neutrophils (band cells), usually indicating acute infection or inflammation.

Front: How is the "Mentzer Index" used to differentiate Iron Deficiency from Thalassemia?

Back: $MCV / RBC count$. If $< 13$, it suggests Thalassemia; if $> 13$, it suggests Iron Deficiency.

Front: Meaning of "Anisocytosis" on a lab report?

Back: Variation in RBC size (measured by RDW - Red Cell Distribution Width).

Front: Meaning of "Poikilocytosis"?

Back: Variation in RBC shape.

Section 16: Specialized Hemolytic States & Inclusions

Front: Mechanism of Parvovirus B19 induced anemia?

Back: Directly infects and lyses Erythroid Precursor cells; causes Aplastic Crisis in patients with high RBC turnover (e.g., Sickle Cell).

Front: Hallmark peripheral smear finding in Microangiopathic Hemolytic Anemia (MAHA)?

Back: Schistocytes (Helmet cells).

Front: What causes the "Starry Sky" appearance in Burkitt Lymphoma histology?

Back: Tingible body macrophages (white "stars") consuming apoptotic B-cells (dark "sky").

Front: Which enzyme is deficient in Methemoglobinemia?

Back: Cytochrome $b_5$ reductase (Methemoglobin reductase).

Front: Treatment for symptomatic Methemoglobinemia?

Back: Methylene blue (reduces $Fe^{3+}$ back to $Fe^{2+}$).

Front: Clinical "classic" presentation of Methemoglobinemia?

Back: Cyanosis (chocolate-colored blood) that does not improve with supplemental Oxygen.

Section 17: Bone Marrow & Myeloproliferative Nuances

Front: Diagnostic hallmark of Myelodysplastic Syndromes (MDS)?

Back: "Ringed Sideroblasts" and "Pseudo-Pelger-Huët anomaly" (bilobed neutrophils).

Front: Major risk of Myelodysplastic Syndrome (MDS)?

Back: Transformation into Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML).

Front: In Polycythemia Vera, what happens to Serum Erythropoietin (EPO) levels?

Back: EPO is low (negative feedback from high RBC mass).

Front: Difference between "Primary" and "Secondary" Polycythemia?

Back: Primary (PV) has low EPO; Secondary (Altitude, Smoking, RCC) has high EPO.

Front: Term for marrow being replaced by "non-hematopoietic" material (tumor, granuloma)?

Back: Myelophthisis.

Section 18: Pediatric & Hereditary Hematology

Front: Most common cause of Hydrops Fetalis?

Back: $\alpha$-Thalassemia (4-gene deletion; Hb Barts / $\gamma_4$).

Front: Most common clinical presentation of Diamond-Blackfan Anemia?

Back: Rapid-onset anemia in the 1st year of life, short stature, and craniofacial/thumb anomalies.

Front: Genetic defect in Fanconi Anemia?

Back: DNA cross-link repair defect; leads to marrow failure and increased risk of AML.

Front: Typical physical findings in Fanconi Anemia?

Back: Cafe-au-lait spots, short stature, and absent/hypoplastic thumbs.

Front: HbH disease involves how many $\alpha$-globin gene deletions?

Back: 3 gene deletions ($\beta_4$ tetramers).

Section 19: High-Yield Clinical Signs

Front: What is "Extramedullary Hematopoiesis"?

Back: RBC production outside the marrow (Spleen/Liver), often seen in Thalassemia or Myelofibrosis.

Front: Pathogenesis of "Bony Bossing" in Thalassemia?

Back: Reactive marrow hyperplasia expanding the marrow space, leading to "Chipmunk facies."
