Back: Triggers insertion of Aquaporin-2 channels into the apical membrane to increase water permeability.

Front: Electrolyte hallmark of "Fanconi-Bickel Syndrome"?

Back: Proximal tubular dysfunction combined with glycogen storage issues (GLUT2 mutation).

LII. Rare Deposition & Systemic Renal Pathology

Front: Renal pathology seen in "Light Chain Deposition Disease" (LCDD)?

Back: Nodular glomerulosclerosis (resembling Kimmelstiel-Wilson) but Congo Red negative.

Front: What is "Cryoglobulinemic Glomerulonephritis"?

Back: A Type II or III MPGN-like pattern often associated with Hepatitis C.

Front: Characteristic EM finding in Cryoglobulinemic GN?

Back: Subendothelial deposits with "curvilinear" or "fingerprint" patterns.

Front: Type of amyloidosis associated with chronic inflammatory diseases (e.g., RA, IBD)?

Back: AA Amyloid (Serum Amyloid A).

Front: Location of "Medullary Nephrocalcinosis" on imaging?

Back: Calcium deposits within the renal pyramids.

LIII. Microbiology & Infections of the Urinary Tract

Front: Primary virulence factor for Uropathogenic E. coli (UPEC)?

Back: P-fimbriae (allow adherence to urothelium).

Front: Organism associated with "Struvite" stones that produces a "swarming" pattern on agar?

Back: Proteus mirabilis.

Front: Most common cause of fungal UTI in catheterized or diabetic patients?

Back: Candida albicans.

Front: Classic renal complication of "Secondary Syphilis"?

Back: Membranous Nephropathy.

Front: Effect of "Schistosoma haematobium" on bladder cytology?

Back: Causes squamous metaplasia leading to Squamous Cell Carcinoma.

LIV. Advanced Clinical Indices & Formulas

Front: Mnemonic for "High Anion Gap" Metabolic Acidosis?

Back: GOLD MARK (Glycols, Oxoproline, L-lactate, D-lactate, Methanol, Aspirin, Renal failure, Ketoacidosis).

Front: What does "FENA > 2%" indicate?

Back: Intrinsic renal damage (e.g., ATN) where the kidney cannot reabsorb sodium.

Front: Formula for "Creatinine Clearance" ($C_{Cr}$)?

Back: $C = (U \times V) / P$ (Urine concentration $\times$ Urine flow rate / Plasma concentration).

Front: Relationship between Serum Potassium and $pH$?

Back: Acidosis leads to hyperkalemia (intercellular exchange of $H^+$ for $K^+$).

LV. Urothelial & Scrotal-Renal Links

Front: Significance of "Hydrocele" in a patient with a renal mass?

Back: Can indicate lymphatic obstruction or a reactive process to a nearby tumor.

Front: Most common location for "Ectopic Ureteral" insertion in males?

Back: Prostatic urethra or seminal vesicles.

Front: Definition of "Vesicoureteral Reflux" (VUR) Grade V?

Back: Severe dilation and tortuosity of the ureter with significant blunting of calyces.

Front: Primary risk factor for "Transitional Cell Carcinoma" of the renal pelvis?

Back: Smoking and exposure to phenacetin.

LVI. Final High-Yield Integration

Front: Difference between "Primary" and "Secondary" Hyperoxaluria?

Back: Primary is a liver enzyme defect (glyoxylate metabolism); Secondary is usually dietary or malabsorption.

Front: Most common renal side effect of "Gold salts" or "Penicillamine"?

Back: Membranous Nephropathy.

Front: What is the "White Line of Toldt"?

Back: A surgical landmark for reflecting the colon to access the kidney (useful for surgery/anatomy-pathology crossover).

Front: Cause of "Toxemic" (Preeclamptic) renal changes?

Back: Placental ischemia leads to the release of sFlt-1, which neutralizes VEGF, damaging glomerular endothelium.

Front: Effect of "Hypokalemia" on the EKG?

Back: U-waves and flattened T-waves.

Front: Effect of "Hyperkalemia" on the EKG?

Back: Peaked T-waves, widened QRS, and loss of P-waves.

=====================================================================
=====================================================================


Respiratory System:


Front: Embryologic origin of the respiratory diverticulum (lung bud)?

Back: Outgrowth of the foregut endoderm.

Front: Structure that separates the respiratory bud from the esophagus?

Back: Tracheoesophageal septum.

Front: Most common type of Tracheoesophageal Fistula (TEF)?

Back: Esophageal atresia with distal TEF (85% of cases).

Front: Clinical presentation of a neonate with TEF?

Back: Drooling, choking, and cyanosis with first feeding.

Front: Stages of lung development in chronological order?

Back: Pseudoglandular, Canalicular, Saccular, Alveolar.

Front: Lung development stage where respiration becomes possible?

Back: Canalicular stage (weeks 16–25).

Front: Cell type responsible for secreting pulmonary surfactant?

Back: Type II pneumocytes.

Front: Primary component of pulmonary surfactant?

Back: Dipalmitoylphosphatidylcholine (Lecithin).

Front: Used to screen for fetal lung maturity in amniotic fluid?

Back: Lecithin-to-sphingomyelin (L/S) ratio > 2.0.

Front: Anatomical location of the horizontal fissure (right lung)?

Back: Level of the 4th rib.

Front: Relationship of the pulmonary artery to the bronchus in the Left lung hilum?

Back: Superior (mnemonic: RALS - Right Anterior, Left Superior).

Front: Most common site for aspirated foreign bodies?

Back: Right lower lobe (due to wider, shorter, more vertical bronchus).

Front: Structures piercing the diaphragm at T10?

Back: Esophagus and Vagus nerves.

Front: Vertebral level where the IVC passes through the diaphragm?

Back: T8.

Front: Muscles used for quiet inspiration?

Back: Diaphragm and external intercostals.

II. Physiology & Mechanics

Front: Definition of Residual Volume (RV)?

Back: Air remaining in lungs after maximal expiration.

Front: Formula for Functional Residual Capacity (FRC)?

Back: $FRC = ERV + RV$.

Front: Changes in FRC in a patient with emphysema?

Back: FRC increases due to loss of elastic recoil.

Front: Definition of physiologic dead space?

Back: Total volume of gas not participating in gas exchange (Anatomic + Alveolar).

Front: Effect of exercise on the hemoglobin-oxygen dissociation curve?

Back: Right shift (increased $H^+$, $CO_2$, temperature, and 2,3-BPG).

Front: Form in which the majority of $CO_2$ is transported in the blood?

Back: Bicarbonate ($HCO_3^-$).

Front: Enzyme that converts $CO_2$ and $H_2O$ to $H_2CO_3$ in RBCs?

Back: Carbonic anhydrase.

Front: Definition of the Haldane effect?

Back: Deoxygenation of blood increases its ability to carry $CO_2$.

Front: High altitude compensation: Erythropoietin levels?

Back: Increased (leads to polycythemia).

Front: Perfusion vs. Ventilation at the apex of the lung?

Back: Both are lower than the base, but $V/Q$ is higher (approx. 3.0).

Front: Response of pulmonary vasculature to hypoxia?

Back: Hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction (shunts blood to ventilated areas).

III. Obstructive Lung Diseases

Front: Cardinal PFT finding in obstructive lung disease?

Back: Decreased $FEV_1/FVC$ ratio (< 0.7).

Front: Reid Index definition?

Back: Ratio of the thickness of the mucous gland layer to the thickness of the wall (epithelium to cartilage).

Front: Diagnostic Reid Index value for Chronic Bronchitis?

Back: > 50%.

Front: Pathogenesis of Centriacinar emphysema?

Back: Smoking-induced inflammation and protease release.

Front: Genetic deficiency associated with Panacinar emphysema?

Back: Alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency.

Front: Classic physical exam finding in emphysema ("Pink Puffers")?

Back: Pursed-lip breathing to maintain airway pressure.

Front: Charcot-Leyden crystals in sputum represent?

Back: Eosinophilic breakdown (Asthma).

Front: Curschmann spirals are found in which condition?

Back: Asthma (whorled mucous plugs).

Front: Permanent dilation of bronchi due to chronic necrotizing infection?

Back: Bronchiectasis.

Front: Primary ciliary dyskinesia with situs inversus?

Back: Kartagener Syndrome.

Front: Common cause of bronchiectasis in a child with malabsorption?

Back: Cystic Fibrosis.

IV. Restrictive Lung Diseases

Front: PFT findings in Restrictive Lung Disease?

Back: Decreased TLC, decreased FVC, normal or increased $FEV_1/FVC$ ratio.

Front: Pathological hallmark of Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis?

Back: Honeycomb lung (subpleural fibrosis).

Front: Standard medication used to slow progression of IPF?

Back: Pirfenidone or Nintedanib.

Front: Histologic finding in Sarcoidosis?

Back: Non-caseating granulomas.

Front: Common chest X-ray finding in Sarcoidosis?

Back: Bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy.

Front: Serum marker often elevated in Sarcoidosis?

Back: Angiotensin-converting enzyme (ACE).

Front: Asteroid bodies and Schaumann bodies are seen in?

Back: Sarcoidosis.

Front: Pneumoconiosis associated with shipyards and insulation?

Back: Asbestosis.

Front: Characteristic pleural finding in Asbestosis?

Back: Calcified pleural plaques.

Front: Most common malignancy associated with asbestos exposure?

Back: Bronchogenic carcinoma (more common than mesothelioma).

Front: Pneumoconiosis showing "eggshell" calcification of hilar nodes?

Back: Silicosis.

Front: Silicosis increases the risk of which infection?

Back: Tuberculosis (impairs macrophage function).

Front: Pneumoconiosis associated with aerospace and manufacturing?

Back: Berylliosis (mimics sarcoidosis).

V. Vascular & Circulatory

Front: Definition of Pulmonary Hypertension?

Back: Mean pulmonary artery pressure $\ge 20$ mmHg at rest.

Front: Gene mutation associated with primary (idiopathic) pulmonary arterial hypertension?

Back: $BMPR2$ (leads to smooth muscle proliferation).

Front: Most common source of a Pulmonary Embolism (PE)?

Back: Deep vein thrombosis (DVT) from proximal leg veins.

Front: Classic EKG finding in PE (though insensitive)?

Back: S1Q3T3.

Front: Gold standard for diagnosing Pulmonary Embolism?

Back: CT Pulmonary Angiography (CTPA).

Front: Virchow’s Triad components?

Back: Stasis, Hypercoagulability, Endothelial injury.

Front: Lines of Zahn indicate?

Back: Thrombus formed before death (alternating layers of platelets/fibrin and RBCs).

Front: Most common cause of community-acquired pneumonia (CAP)?

Back: Streptococcus pneumoniae.

Front: "Currant jelly" sputum is associated with?

Back: Klebsiella pneumoniae.

Front: Common cause of pneumonia in CF patients and intubated patients?

Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa.

Front: Cause of "walking pneumonia" with cold agglutinins?

Back: Mycoplasma pneumoniae.

Front: Pneumonia associated with air conditioning or water towers?

Back: Legionella pneumophila.

Front: Stain used to identify Mycobacterium tuberculosis?

Back: Ziehl-Neelsen (Acid-fast) stain.

Front: Definition of a Ghon complex?

Back: Ghon focus (subpleural granuloma) plus hilar lymph node involvement.

Front: Location in the lung most commonly affected by Secondary TB?

Back: Apex (high oxygen tension).

Front: Primary treatment for active TB (RIPE)?

Back: Rifampin, Isoniazid, Pyrazinamide, Ethambutol.

Front: Side effect of Ethambutol?

Back: Optic neuritis (red-green color blindness).

Front: Opportunistic pneumonia in AIDS with "ground glass" opacities?

Back: Pneumocystis jirovecii.

VII. Neoplasia

Front: Most common lung cancer in non-smokers?

Back: Adenocarcinoma.

Front: Lung cancer associated with hypercalcemia (PTHrP)?

Back: Squamous cell carcinoma.

Front: Central lung cancer showing "keratin pearls" histologically?

Back: Squamous cell carcinoma.

Front: Highly aggressive neuroendocrine lung tumor?

Back: Small cell carcinoma.

Front: Small cell carcinoma is associated with which paraneoplastic syndromes?

Back: ACTH (Cushing), ADH (SIADH), Lambert-Eaton.

Front: Lung cancer associated with $EGFR$ or $ALK$ mutations?

Back: Adenocarcinoma.

Front: Definition of a Pancoast tumor?

Back: Superior sulcus tumor that can cause Horner Syndrome.

Front: Symptoms of Horner Syndrome?

Back: Ptosis, Miosis, Anhydrosis.

Front: Tumor of the pleura associated with asbestos?

Back: Mesothelioma.

VIII. Critical Care & Miscellaneous

Front: Pathogenesis of ARDS?

Back: Diffuse alveolar damage (DAD) leading to hyaline membrane formation.

Front: Characteristic ABG finding in early Pulmonary Embolism?

Back: Respiratory alkalosis (due to hyperventilation).

Front: Definition of a Tension Pneumothorax?

Back: Air enters pleural space but cannot exit, causing mediastinal shift.

Front: Direction of tracheal deviation in Tension Pneumothorax?

Back: Away from the side of the lesion.

Front: Definition of Chylothorax?

Back: Pleural effusion containing lymph (milky appearance), usually from thoracic duct injury.

Front: Light’s Criteria: Protein ratio for exudative effusion?

Back: Pleural fluid protein / Serum protein > 0.5.

Front: Obstructive Sleep Apnea (OSA) is associated with?

Back: Obesity, loud snoring, daytime somnolence, and systemic hypertension.

Front: First-line treatment for OSA?

Back: CPAP (Continuous Positive Airway Pressure).

Front: Effect of Carbon Monoxide (CO) on the oxygen dissociation curve?

Back: Left shift (inhibits oxygen unloading).

Front: Physical exam finding in CO poisoning?

Back: "Cherry-red" skin discoloration (rarely seen in living patients).

Front: Treatment for Cyanide poisoning?

Back: Nitrites (to induce methemoglobin), Thiosulfate, or Hydroxocobalamin.

Front: Difference between Hypoxemia and Hypoxia?

Back: Hypoxemia is low $PaO_2$; Hypoxia is low $O_2$ delivery to tissues.

Front: Formula for A-a gradient?

Back: $PAO_2 - PaO_2$.

Front: Causes of hypoxemia with a NORMAL A-a gradient?

Back: Hypoventilation and High Altitude.

Front: Mechanism of action of Albuterol?

Back: $\beta_2$-agonist (bronchodilation).

Front: Mechanism of action of Tiotropium?

Back: Long-acting Muscarinic Antagonist (LAMA).

Front: Mechanism of action of Montelukast?

Back: Leukotriene receptor antagonist.

Front: Major side effect of inhaled corticosteroids?

Back: Oral candidiasis (thrush).

Front: Drug of choice for immediate relief of acute asthma symptoms?

Back: Short-acting $\beta_2$-agonist (SABA).

Front: First-line long-term therapy for persistent asthma?

Back: Inhaled corticosteroids (ICS).

Front: Definition of Compliance?

Back: Change in volume divided by change in pressure ($\Delta V / \Delta P$).

Front: Condition characterized by pathologically increased lung compliance?

Back: Emphysema.

Front: Condition characterized by pathologically decreased lung compliance?

Back: Pulmonary fibrosis.

Front: Primary factor determining airway resistance?

Back: Airway radius (Resistance $\propto 1/r^4$).

Front: Part of the tracheobronchial tree with the highest individual airway resistance?

Back: Medium-sized bronchi (generations 2–5).

Front: Part of the tracheobronchial tree with the lowest total resistance?

Back: Terminal bronchioles (due to massive parallel arrangement).

Front: Equal Pressure Point (EPP) in healthy lungs during forced expiration?

Back: Occurs in cartilaginous airways to prevent collapse.

Front: Shift of the Equal Pressure Point in emphysema?

Back: Moves distally toward smaller, non-cartilaginous airways (leading to airway collapse).

Front: Primary determinant of $PAO_2$ (Alveolar Oxygen)?

Back: The Alveolar Gas Equation: $PAO_2 = FiO_2(P_{atm} - PH_2O) - (PaCO_2 / R)$.

Front: Normal value for the A-a gradient in a young adult?

Back: 5–15 mmHg.

Front: Calculation for the age-adjusted upper limit of normal A-a gradient?

Back: $(Age / 4) + 4$.

Front: Only cause of hypoxemia that cannot be corrected with 100% $O_2$?

Back: Right-to-Left Shunt.

Front: Oxygen content formula ($CaO_2$)?

Back: $(1.34 \times Hb \times SaO_2) + (0.003 \times PaO_2)$.

X. Detailed Pathology (Robbins Focus)

Front: Microscopic appearance of "Heart Failure Cells"?

Back: Hemosiderin-laden macrophages in the alveoli.

Front: Pathogenesis of "Brown Induration" of the lungs?

Back: Chronic pulmonary congestion due to left-sided heart failure.

Front: Histologic hallmark of Goodpasture Syndrome?

Back: Linear IgG deposits along the alveolar basement membrane.

Front: Classic triad of Wegener Granulomatosis (Granulomatosis with Polyangiitis)?

Back: Focal necrotizing vasculitis, necrotizing granulomas of the lung, and necrotizing glomerulonephritis.

Front: Serological marker for Granulomatosis with Polyangiitis?

Back: c-ANCA (PR3-ANCA).

Front: Serological marker for Churg-Strauss Syndrome (Eosinophilic Granulomatosis with Polyangiitis)?

Back: p-ANCA (MPO-ANCA).

Front: Type of hypersensitivity reaction in Hypersensitivity Pneumonitis?

Back: Mixed Type III and Type IV.

Front: Common name for Hypersensitivity Pneumonitis caused by actinomycetes in hay?

Back: Farmer’s Lung.

Front: Most common location for Intralobar Sequestration?

Back: Left lower lobe.

Front: Difference between Sequestration and normal lung tissue?

Back: Sequestration lacks communication with the tracheobronchial tree and has systemic arterial supply.

Front: Definition of Resorption Atelectasis?

Back: Lung collapse due to complete obstruction of an airway.

Front: Most common cause of Compression Atelectasis?

Back: Accumulation of fluid (effusion), blood, or air within the pleural cavity.

XI. Detailed Microbiology & Clinical Presentations

Front: Pneumonia in an alcoholic with "Current Jelly" sputum?

Back: Klebsiella pneumoniae.

Front: Common cause of pneumonia following a viral influenza infection?

Back: Staphylococcus aureus.

Front: Only DNA virus causing lower respiratory tract infections in children?

Back: Adenovirus.

Front: Major virulence factor of Streptococcus pneumoniae?

Back: Polysaccharide capsule.

Front: Lab finding in Legionella pneumonia?

Back: Hyponatremia (low sodium).

Front: Culture medium required for Legionella pneumophila?

Back: Charcoal Yeast Extract (CYE) agar buffered with iron and cysteine.

Front: Morphological stage of Lobar Pneumonia characterized by fibrinous exudate and RBCs?

Back: Red Hepatization.

Front: Morphological stage of Lobar Pneumonia where RBCs disintegrate but fibrin remains?

Back: Gray Hepatization.

Front: Common cause of Nosocomial (hospital-acquired) pneumonia?

Back: Pseudomonas aeruginosa or MRSA.

Front: Primary risk factor for developing a Lung Abscess?

Back: Aspiration (often due to alcoholism, epilepsy, or poor dentition).

Front: Most common organisms isolated from Lung Abscesses?

Back: Anaerobes (Bacteroides, Fusobacterium, Peptostreptococcus).

XII. Pharmacology (Standard Board Prep)

Front: Mechanism of action of Theophylline?

Back: Phosphodiesterase inhibition (increases cAMP) and adenosine receptor antagonism.

Front: Major limiting side effect of Theophylline?

Back: Narrow therapeutic index (cardiotoxicity and neurotoxicity/seizures).

Front: Mechanism of action of Cromolyn sodium?

Back: Stabilizes mast cell membranes to prevent degranulation.

Front: Monoclonal antibody used in severe, refractory asthma?

Back: Omalizumab.

Front: Target of Omalizumab?

Back: IgE (binds unbound IgE to prevent mast cell sensitization).

Front: Mechanism of Mepolizumab?

Back: IL-5 antagonist (inhibits eosinophil recruitment).

Front: Drug of choice for treating exercise-induced bronchospasm?

Back: Albuterol (SABA).

Front: First-line treatment for a COPD exacerbation?

Back: SABA (Albuterol) + SAMA (Ipratropium).

Front: Use of Guaifenesin?

Back: Expectorant (thins respiratory secretions).

Front: Mechanism of action of N-acetylcysteine in CF patients?

Back: Mucolytic (breaks disulfide bonds in mucus).

Front: Antidote for Acetaminophen toxicity?

Back: N-acetylcysteine (replenishes glutathione).

Front: Primary toxicity of Isoniazid?

Back: Hepatotoxicity and peripheral neuropathy (B6 deficiency).

Front: Reason for adding Vitamin B6 (Pyridoxine) to Isoniazid therapy?

Back: To prevent peripheral neuropathy.

Front: Drug causing "Red-Orange" discoloration of body fluids?

Back: Rifampin.

XIII. Radiography & Physical Exam Signs

Front: Radiographic sign for Croup (Laryngotracheobronchitis)?

Back: Steeple Sign (subglottic narrowing).

Front: Radiographic sign for Epiglottitis?

Back: Thumbprint Sign (swollen epiglottis).

Front: Most common cause of Epiglottitis?

Back: Haemophilus influenzae type b (Hib).

Front: Physical exam finding: Increased tactile fremitus?

Back: Consolidation (e.g., Lobar Pneumonia).

Front: Physical exam finding: Decreased tactile fremitus with hyperresonance?

Back: Pneumothorax or Emphysema.

Front: Physical exam finding: Decreased tactile fremitus with dullness to percussion?

Back: Pleural Effusion.

Front: Definition of Egophony?

Back: "E" to "A" change heard over areas of lung consolidation.

Front: Appearance of Pulmonary Edema on Chest X-ray?

Back: Kerley B lines, cephalization of vessels, and perihilar "Batwing" opacities.

Front: Definition of the "Westermark Sign" on CXR?

Back: Regional oligemia (decreased vascularity) distal to a pulmonary embolism.

Front: Definition of "Hampton’s Hump" on CXR?

Back: Shallow, wedge-shaped opacification in the periphery (represents lung infarction).

XIV. Respiratory Acid-Base

Front: Primary disturbance in Respiratory Acidosis?

Back: Increased $PaCO_2$ (> 40 mmHg).

Front: Common causes of Respiratory Alkalosis?

Back: Hyperventilation (anxiety, PE, high altitude, salicylate poisoning early).

Front: Renal compensation for chronic respiratory acidosis?

Back: Increased $HCO_3^-$ reabsorption and $H^+$ excretion.

Front: Metabolic disturbance in Salicylate (Aspirin) overdose?

Back: Mixed Respiratory Alkalosis and Anion Gap Metabolic Acidosis.

Front: Histologic finding in Small Cell Carcinoma?

Back: Small blue cells with "Azzopardi effect" (DNA staining of vessel walls).

Front: Most common lung cancer associated with non-smokers and $EGFR$ mutations?

Back: Adenocarcinoma.

Front: Histologic appearance of Bronchioloalveolar Carcinoma (Adenocarcinoma in situ)?

Back: Tall, columnar cells growing along existing alveolar septa (lepidic growth).

Front: Definition of a Hamartoma in the lung?

Back: Benign tumor composed of "disorganized" lung tissue, often containing cartilage and fat.

Front: Radiographic appearance of a Hamartoma?

Back: "Popcorn" calcification on CT or CXR.

Front: Most common primary site for "cannonball" metastases to the lung?

Back: Renal cell carcinoma or Choriocarcinoma.

Front: Definition of a "Pleural Tail" on imaging?

Back: Linear opacity extending from a peripheral lung nodule to the visceral pleura.

Front: Pathogenesis of Silicotic nodules?

Back: Macrophages ingest silica, die, and release fibrogenic factors.

Front: Major risk factor for developing Mesothelioma?

Back: Asbestos exposure (long latency period of 25–40 years).

Front: Histologic marker for Mesothelioma?

Back: Calretinin positive and long, slender microvilli on electron microscopy.

XVI. West's Respiratory Physiology: Gas Exchange & Transport

Front: Definition of Diffusing Capacity ($DL_{CO}$)?

Back: Measurement of the lung's ability to transfer gas from inspired air to RBCs using Carbon Monoxide.

Front: Condition that increases $DL_{CO}$?

Back: Alveolar hemorrhage (e.g., Goodpasture) or polycythemia.

Front: Conditions that decrease $DL_{CO}$?

Back: Emphysema, Pulmonary Fibrosis, and Pulmonary Embolism.

Front: Oxygen transport: Definition of Methemoglobinemia?

Back: Iron in hemoglobin is in the $Fe^{3+}$ (ferric) state instead of $Fe^{2+}$ (ferrous).

Front: Classic clinical sign of Methemoglobinemia?

Back: Cyanosis unresponsive to supplemental oxygen and "chocolate-colored" blood.

Front: Treatment for Methemoglobinemia?

Back: Methylene blue or Vitamin C.

Front: Definition of the Bohr Effect?

Back: Increased $CO_2$ and decreased pH lower hemoglobin's affinity for $O_2$.

Front: Effect of 2,3-BPG on the Oxygen-Hemoglobin dissociation curve?

Back: Stabilizes the T-state (deoxygenated) and shifts the curve to the right.

Front: Respiratory center responsible for the rhythm of breathing?

Back: Pre-Bötzinger complex in the medulla.

Front: Primary stimulus for central chemoreceptors?

Back: $H^+$ concentration in the brain extracellular fluid (derived from arterial $CO_2$).

Front: Primary stimulus for peripheral chemoreceptors (Carotid/Aortic bodies)?

Back: Decreased $PaO_2$ (< 60 mmHg).

XVII. Clinical Syndromes & Emergencies

Front: Definition of Sleep Apnea-Hypopnea Syndrome?

Back: Repeated episodes of obstructive or central airflow cessation during sleep.

Front: Common complication of untreated Obstructive Sleep Apnea?

Back: Pulmonary hypertension and Cor Pulmonale.

Front: Clinical triad of Fat Embolism Syndrome?

Back: Hypoxemia, neurologic abnormalities, and petechial rash.

Front: Common trigger for Fat Embolism?

Back: Fracture of long bones (e.g., femur).

Front: Definition of Scimitar Syndrome?

Back: Anomalous pulmonary venous return from the right lung to the IVC.

Front: Classic presentation of Loffler Syndrome?

Back: Transient pulmonary opacities and eosinophilia, often due to parasitic migration.

Front: Primary treatment for Anaphylaxis causing airway obstruction?

Back: Intramuscular Epinephrine.

Front: Definition of Kartagener Syndrome?

Back: Triad of Situs inversus, chronic sinusitis, and bronchiectasis.

Front: Underlying defect in Kartagener Syndrome?

Back: Dynein arm defect in cilia.

XVIII. Detailed Pharmacology (High-Yield Additions)

Front: Mechanism of action of Bosentan?

Back: Endothelin-1 receptor antagonist (used for Pulmonary HTN).

Front: Mechanism of action of Sildenafil in pulmonary context?

Back: PDE-5 inhibitor; increases cGMP to cause vasodilation.

Front: Mechanism of action of Epoprostenol?

Back: Prostacyclin ($PGI_2$) analog with direct vasodilatory effects.

Front: Drug used to treat respiratory depression in opioid overdose?

Back: Naloxone (Mu-opioid receptor antagonist).

Front: Major side effect of Bleomycin?

Back: Pulmonary fibrosis.

Front: Major side effect of Amiodarone regarding the lungs?

Back: Chronic interstitial pneumonitis (Pulmonary fibrosis).

Front: Mechanism of action of Dextromethorphan?

Back: Antitussive; NMDA receptor antagonist.

Front: First-line treatment for Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia?

Back: Trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole (TMP-SMX).

XIX. Physical Exam & Diagnostics

Front: Sound heard on auscultation in Pleuritis?

Back: Pleural friction rub.

Front: Definition of Bronchophony?

Back: Patient's voice remains loud and distinct over areas of consolidation.

Front: Sign of a large Pleural Effusion on physical exam?

Back: Tracheal deviation away from the affected side.

Front: Sign of massive Atelectasis on physical exam?

Back: Tracheal deviation toward the affected side.

Front: Meaning of "Percussion Dullness" at the lung base?

Back: Presence of fluid (effusion) or solid (consolidation/tumor).

Front: Meaning of "Hyperresonance" on percussion?

Back: Excess air in the pleural space (pneumothorax) or lungs (emphysema).

Front: Primary clearance mechanism for particles < 2 micrometers in the alveoli?

Back: Alveolar macrophages (mucociliary escalator only goes to terminal bronchioles).

Front: Protein deficiency in Kartagener syndrome?

Back: Dynein arms (structural component of microtubule doublets).

Front: Major chemotactic factor for neutrophils in the lung?

Back: Interleukin-8 (IL-8).

Front: Cell type that proliferates to repair alveolar epithelium after Type I pneumocyte injury?

Back: Type II pneumocytes.

Front: Primary antioxidant enzyme in the lung?

Back: Superoxide dismutase.

XXI. Rare Pathologies & Specialized Syndromes

Front: Definition of Pulmonary Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis?

Back: Smoking-related interstitial lung disease characterized by "Birbeck granules" (tennis racket shape) on EM.

Front: Classic radiographic finding in Pulmonary Alveolar Proteinosis?

Back: "Crazy paving" pattern (ground-glass opacities with thickened interlobular septa).

Front: Treatment for Pulmonary Alveolar Proteinosis?

Back: Whole-lung lavage.

Front: Definition of Lymphangioleiomyomatosis (LAM)?

Back: Cystic lung disease in women of childbearing age associated with tuberous sclerosis.

Front: Marker used to identify LAM cells?

Back: HMB-45 (a melanoma marker).

Front: Cause of "Inhalation Anthrax"?

Back: Bacillus anthracis (presents with widened mediastinum on CXR).

Front: Pathogenesis of Alpha-1 Antitrypsin (AAT) deficiency-related liver disease?

Back: Misfolding of the AAT protein (PiZ allele) which accumulates in the ER of hepatocytes.

XXII. Advanced Physiology & Gas Laws

Front: Meaning of a "Left Shift" in the P50 of hemoglobin?

Back: Hemoglobin has a higher affinity for $O_2$ (holds on tighter).

Front: Boyle’s Law application in the lungs?

Back: Pressure and volume are inversely proportional; used to measure FRC via plethysmography.

Front: Definition of "Closing Volume"?

Back: The volume at which the airways in the dependent (lower) parts of the lung begin to close.

Front: Effect of aging on Closing Volume?

Back: Closing volume increases with age.

Front: Definition of Physiological Shunt ($Q_s/Q_t$)?

Back: The fraction of the total cardiac output that traverses the systemic circulation without being oxygenated.

XXIII. High-Yield Microbiology & Fungal Infections

Front: Fungal pneumonia common in the Southwestern US (California/Arizona)?

Back: Coccidioides immitis.

Front: Microscopic hallmark of Coccidioides in tissue?

Back: Spherules filled with endospores.

Front: Fungal pneumonia associated with bird or bat droppings (Ohio/Mississippi River Valleys)?

Back: Histoplasma capsulatum.

Front: Microscopic hallmark of Histoplasma?

Back: Small yeast cells residing within macrophages.

Front: Fungal pneumonia with "broad-based budding" yeast?

Back: Blastomyces dermatitidis.

Front: Opportunistic fungus causing a "fungus ball" (Aspergilloma) in pre-existing cavities?

Back: Aspergillus fumigatus.

Front: Condition characterized by hypersensitivity to Aspergillus in asthma/CF patients?

Back: Allergic Bronchopulmonary Aspergillosis (ABPA).

XXIV. Acid-Base & Ventilation-Perfusion

Front: Formula for Alveolar Ventilation ($V_A$)?

Back: $V_A = (Tidal Volume - Dead Space) \times Respiratory Rate$.

Front: Effect of Pulmonary Embolism on Dead Space?

Back: Increases physiological dead space (ventilation without perfusion).

Front: Calculation of Anion Gap?

Back: $Na^+ - (Cl^- + HCO_3^-)$.

Front: Normal Anion Gap value?

Back: 8 to 12 mEq/L.

Front: Winter’s Formula for respiratory compensation in metabolic acidosis?

Back: $Expected PaCO_2 = 1.5[HCO_3^-] + 8 \pm 2$.

XXV. Pharmacology: Extra High-Yield

Front: Mechanism of action of Varenicline?

Back: Nicotinic ACh receptor partial agonist (used for smoking cessation).

Front: Mechanism of action of Benralizumab?

Back: IL-5 receptor alpha antagonist (depletes eosinophils).

Front: Drug used to prevent Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) in high-risk infants?

Back: Palivizumab (monoclonal antibody against RSV F protein).

Front: Effect of Beta-blockers (non-selective) on asthmatic patients?

Back: Bronchoconstriction (blocks $\beta_2$ receptors).

XXVI. Clinical Pearls & Physical Diagnosis

Front: Definition of "Kussmaul breathing"?

Back: Deep, labored breathing pattern often associated with severe metabolic acidosis (DKA).

Front: Definition of "Cheyne-Stokes respiration"?

Back: Cycles of crescendo-decrescendo breathing followed by apnea (seen in heart failure or stroke).

Front: Significance of a pleural fluid pH < 7.2?

Back: Suggests an empyema or complicated parapneumonic effusion (requires drainage).

Front: Significance of "pulsus paradoxus" in status asthmaticus?

Back: Drop in systolic BP > 10 mmHg during inspiration; indicates severe airway obstruction.

Front: Most common cause of superior vena cava (SVC) syndrome?

Back: Lung cancer (especially Small Cell Carcinoma).

Front: Safe zone for needle thoracostomy (tension pneumothorax)?

Back: Second intercostal space at the midclavicular line.

Front: Level for performing a thoracentesis (needle decompression of effusion)?

Back: Between ribs 6 and 8 (midaxillary line) or 8 and 10 (paravertebral line).

Front: Structure most at risk during a thoracentesis if the needle is inserted below the rib?

Back: The intercostal nerve, artery, and vein (VAN bundle).

Front: Proper placement of a chest tube (tube thoracostomy)?

Back: 4th or 5th intercostal space at the anterior axillary line.

Front: Level at which the trachea bifurcates (Carina)?

Back: T4 vertebral level (sternal angle).

XXVIII. Advanced Clinical Pathophysiology

Front: Definition of the "Shunt Equation"?

Back: $Qs/Qt = (CcO_2 - CaO_2) / (CcO_2 - CvO_2)$.

Front: Primary cause of hypoxemia in a patient with a massive pulmonary embolism?

Back: $V/Q$ mismatch (increased dead space).

Front: The "Haldane Effect" describes $CO_2$ release in which organ?

Back: The lungs (binding of $O_2$ to Hb promotes $CO_2$ dissociation).

Front: Definition of "Orthodeoxia"?

Back: Hypoxemia that worsens when moving from a supine to an upright position (seen in Hepatopulmonary Syndrome).

Front: Definition of "Platypnea"?

Back: Dyspnea that worsens when upright.

XXIX. Specific Histopathology & Cytology

Front: Cytological finding in Bronchoalveolar Lavage (BAL) of a patient with Asbestosis?

Back: Ferruginous bodies (asbestos fibers coated with iron).

Front: Histological finding in Silicosis?

Back: Whorled collagenous nodules with peripheral "eggshell" calcification.

Front: Appearance of Coal Worker’s Pneumoconiosis (CWP) on histology?

Back: Carbon-laden macrophages (coal macules) around bronchioles.

Front: Most common location for "Caplan Syndrome" nodules?

Back: Peripheral lung (seen in patients with both Rheumatoid Arthritis and Pneumoconiosis).

XXX. Pulmonary Pharmacology: Toxicity & Interactions

Front: Drug-induced interstitial lung disease: Mechanism of Amiodarone?

Back: Phospholipidosis (accumulation of foamy macrophages in the alveoli).

Front: Respiratory side effect of Methotrexate?

Back: Hypersensitivity pneumonitis (granulomatous reaction).

Front: Mechanism of Nitrofurantoin-induced lung injury?

Back: Direct oxidative damage to lung parenchyma (can be acute or chronic).

Front: Why is the use of 100% Oxygen avoided in patients with Paraquat poisoning?

Back: Oxygen exacerbates the oxidative lung injury caused by Paraquat.

XXXI. High-Yield Pediatric Respiratory Facts

Front: Most common cause of bronchiolitis in infants?

Back: Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV).

Front: Common complication of Neonatal Respiratory Distress Syndrome (NRDS) treatment?

Back: Bronchopulmonary Dysplasia (due to oxygen toxicity and barotrauma).

Front: Most common cause of Stridor in a neonate?

Back: Laryngomalacia (collapsible supraglottic structures).

Front: Character of the cough in Bordetella pertussis?

Back: Paroxysmal cough followed by an inspiratory "whoop."

XXXII. Final "Board-Style" Rapid Fire

Front: Most likely diagnosis: Smoker, hypercalcemia, central lung mass?

Back: Squamous cell carcinoma.

Front: Most likely diagnosis: Non-smoker, female, peripheral lung mass?

Back: Adenocarcinoma.

Front: Most likely diagnosis: Weight loss, night sweats, apical cavitary lesion?

Back: Reactivation (Secondary) Tuberculosis.

Front: Most likely diagnosis: Sudden pleuritic chest pain, tall thin male, hyperresonance?

Back: Spontaneous Pneumothorax.

Front: Most likely diagnosis: 24 hours post-femur fracture, confusion, petechiae?

Back: Fat Embolism Syndrome.

=====================================================================
=====================================================================


Reproductive Pathology:

Front: Most common cause of a Bartholin Cyst?

Back: Inflammation and obstruction of the Bartholin gland duct.

Front: Classic clinical presentation of Lichen Sclerosus?

Back: Thinning of the epidermis (parchment-like) and "leukoplakia" in postmenopausal women.

Front: Risk of progression to Squamous Cell Carcinoma in Lichen Sclerosus?

Back: Slightly increased risk.

Front: Histologic hallmark of Lichen Simplex Chronicus?

Back: Hyperplasia of the vulvar squamous epithelium and dermal inflammation.

Front: Definitive cell type seen in Extramammary Paget Disease?

Back: Large, malignant epithelial cells with PAS+, Keratin+, and S100- staining.

Front: Key distinction between Vulvar Paget Disease and Melanoma?

Back: Paget cells are PAS+ and Keratin+, while Melanoma is S100+.

Front: Most common primary malignancy of the vagina?

Back: Squamous cell carcinoma, usually associated with high-risk HPV.

Front: Classic exposure history for Vaginal Clear Cell Adenocarcinoma?

Back: In utero exposure to Diethylstilbestrol (DES).

Front: Characteristic histologic finding in Sarcoma Botryoides (Rhabdomyosarcoma)?

Back: Spindle-shaped cells with "cambium layer" and skeletal muscle differentiation (desmin+).

Front: Defining feature of the Transformation Zone in the cervix?

Back: Area where squamous epithelium meets columnar epithelium; site of most HPV infections.

Uterus and Endometrium

Front: Primary driver of Endometrial Hyperplasia?

Back: Unopposed estrogen (obesity, PCOS, or exogenous source).

Front: Most common symptom of Endometrial Carcinoma?

Back: Postmenopausal bleeding.

Front: Genetic mutation most associated with Endometrioid Carcinoma (Type I)?

Back: PTEN tumor suppressor gene mutation.

Front: Histologic characteristic of Serous Endometrial Carcinoma (Type II)?

Back: Papillary growth pattern with Psammoma bodies and p53 mutations.

Front: Definition of Adenomyosis?

Back: Extension of endometrial glands and stroma into the uterine myometrium.

Front: Definition of Endometriosis?

Back: Ectopic endometrial glands and stroma outside the uterine cavity.

Front: Most common site for Endometriosis?

Back: The ovaries (often forming "Chocolate Cysts").

Front: Common clinical triad of Endometriosis?

Back: Dysmenorrhea, dyspareunia, and infertility.

Front: Most common benign tumor in females?

Back: Leiomyoma (Fibroid).

Front: Characteristic gross appearance of a Leiomyoma?

Back: Multiple, well-circumscribed, firm, gray-white whorled masses.

Front: Key distinction between Leiomyoma and Leiomyosarcoma?

Back: Leiomyosarcomas are solitary, show necrosis, hemorrhage, and high mitotic activity.

Ovarian Pathology

Front: Most common type of Ovarian Tumor?

Back: Surface epithelial tumors.

Front: Serum marker used to monitor response to treatment in surface epithelial ovarian cancer?

Back: CA-125.

Front: Most common malignant germ cell tumor in children?

Back: Yolk Sac Tumor (Endodermal Sinus Tumor).

Front: Characteristic histologic finding in Yolk Sac Tumors?

Back: Schiller-Duval bodies (glomeruloid-like structures).

Front: Tumor marker elevated in Yolk Sac Tumors?

Back: Alpha-fetoprotein (AFP).

Front: Ovarian counterpart to the male seminoma?

Back: Dysgerminoma.

Front: Most common benign germ cell tumor in young women?

Back: Cystic Teratoma (Dermoid Cyst).

Front: Definition of Struma Ovarii?

Back: A specialized teratoma composed primarily of thyroid tissue.

Front: Most common clinical presentation of a Granulosa Cell Tumor?

Back: Signs of estrogen excess (precocious puberty or postmenopausal bleeding).

Front: Histologic hallmark of Granulosa Cell Tumors?

Back: Call-Exner bodies (small follicles filled with eosinophilic material).

Front: Definition of a Meigs Syndrome?

Back: Triad of ovarian fibroma, ascites, and pleural effusion.

Front: Definition of a Krukenberg Tumor?

Back: Metastatic signet-ring cell carcinoma to the ovaries, usually from a gastric primary.

Gestational and Placental Pathology

Front: Genetic makeup of a Complete Hydatidiform Mole?

Back: 46,XX or 46,XY (all paternal DNA; empty egg fertilized by two sperm or one sperm that duplicates).

Front: Genetic makeup of a Partial Hydatidiform Mole?

Back: Triploid (69,XXX or 69,XXY); normal egg fertilized by two sperm.

Front: "Snowstorm" appearance on ultrasound indicates?

Back: Hydatidiform Mole.

Front: Risk of Choriocarcinoma is higher in which type of mole?

Back: Complete Hydatidiform Mole.

Front: Major hormone elevated in Gestational Trophoblastic Disease?

Back: beta-hCG.

Front: Characteristic of Eclampsia vs. Preeclampsia?

Back: Preeclampsia (hypertension, edema, proteinuria) + Seizures.

Front: Definition of Placenta Previa?

Back: Attachment of the placenta to the lower uterine segment, covering the internal os.

Front: Primary symptom of Placenta Abruptio?

Back: Painful vaginal bleeding in the third trimester.

Male Reproductive: Testis and Prostate

Front: Most common risk factor for Testicular Germ Cell Tumors?

Back: Cryptorchidism (undescended testes).

Front: Most common testicular tumor in men aged 15-40?

Back: Seminoma.

Front: Histology of a Seminoma?

Back: Large cells with clear cytoplasm and central nuclei ("fried egg" appearance) in lobules.

Front: Tumor markers for Choriocarcinoma in males?

Back: Significantly elevated hCG.

Front: Most common testicular tumor in infants and children?

Back: Yolk Sac Tumor.

Front: Defining feature of Reidell’s cells in Leydig Cell Tumors?

Back: Reinke crystals (rod-shaped cytoplasmic inclusions).

Front: Primary location of Benign Prostatic Hyperplasia (BPH)?

Back: Transitional zone (periurethral).

Front: Primary location of Prostatic Adenocarcinoma?

Back: Peripheral zone (posterior lobe).

Front: Use of PSA (Prostate Specific Antigen) in screening?

Back: Nonspecific; can be elevated in BPH, prostatitis, and cancer.

Front: Most common site for Prostate Cancer metastasis?

Back: Bone (Osteoblastic lesions in the lumbar spine).

Breast Pathology

Front: Most common cause of "Green-brown" nipple discharge?

Back: Mammary Duct Ectasia.

Front: Characteristics of Fibrocystic Changes (Non-proliferative)?

Back: Cysts, fibrosis, and apocrine metaplasia (no increased risk of cancer).

Front: Proliferative breast changes that increase cancer risk?

Back: Sclerosing adenosis, ductal hyperplasia, and atypical hyperplasia.

Front: Most common benign tumor of the female breast?

Back: Fibroadenoma.

Front: Characteristic clinical behavior of a Fibroadenoma?

Back: Estrogen-sensitive (grows during pregnancy, shrinks during menopause).

Front: Distinguishing feature of a Phyllodes Tumor from a Fibroadenoma?

Back: "Leaf-like" projections and higher cellularity/malignancy potential.

Front: Location of Intraductal Papilloma?

Back: Within the large lactiferous ducts (typically presents with bloody nipple discharge).

Front: Most common type of invasive breast cancer?

Back: Invasive Ductal Carcinoma (No Special Type).

Front: Histologic feature of Invasive Lobular Carcinoma?

Back: "Single-file" pattern of cells due to loss of E-cadherin.

Front: Clinical presentation of Inflammatory Breast Cancer?

Back: Peau d’orange (orange peel skin) due to lymphatic obstruction.

Front: Significance of Paget Disease of the Nipple?

Back: Indicates underlying Ductal Carcinoma In Situ (DCIS).

Infectious & Inflammatory

Front: Causative agent of Syphilis?

Back: Treponema pallidum.

Front: Characteristic lesion of Primary Syphilis?

Back: Chancre (painless ulcer).

Front: Characteristic lesion of Secondary Syphilis?

Back: Condyloma lata (flat, moist, wart-like lesions).

Front: Causative agent of Lymphogranuloma Venereum?

Back: Chlamydia trachomatis (L1-L3 serotypes).

Front: Most common cause of Pelvic Inflammatory Disease (PID)?

Back: Neisseria gonorrhoeae and Chlamydia trachomatis.

Front: Potential complication of PID involving the liver?

Back: Fitz-Hugh-Curtis Syndrome (violin-string adhesions to the liver capsule).

Front: Diagnostic finding in Bacterial Vaginosis?

Back: "Clue cells" (vaginal epithelial cells covered in bacteria).

Advanced Concepts & Syndromes

Front: Common genetic mutation in Polycystic Ovarian Syndrome (PCOS)?

Back: Hyperinsulinemia leading to deranged LH:FSH ratio (>2:1).

Front: Classic clinical pentad of PCOS?

Back: Obesity, hirsutism, acne, infertility, and oligomenorrhea.

Front: Association of Lichen Sclerosus with other diseases?

Back: Autoimmune disorders (e.g., thyroiditis, vitiligo).

Front: Role of High-risk HPV (16, 18) in oncogenesis?

Back: E6 protein inhibits p53; E7 protein inhibits Rb.

Front: Difference between a follicular cyst and a corpus luteum cyst?

Back: Follicular arises from unruptured follicle; Corpus Luteum arises from a failed involution of the corpus luteum.

Front: Histologic appearance of Medullary Carcinoma of the breast?

Back: Sheets of large cells with prominent lymphocytic infiltrate (associated with BRCA1).

Front: Most common cause of Scrotal Swelling in infants?

Back: Hydrocele (patent processus vaginalis).

Front: Mechanism of a Varicocele?

Back: Dilated veins in the pampiniform plexus ("bag of worms").

Front: Why are Varicoceles more common on the left side?

Back: The left spermatic vein drains into the left renal vein at a 90-degree angle.

Front: Definition of Hypospadias?

Back: Urethral opening on the ventral (bottom) surface of the penis.

Front: Definition of Epispadias?

Back: Urethral opening on the dorsal (top) surface of the penis (associated with bladder exstrophy).

Front: Definition of Bowen Disease of the penis?

Back: In situ Squamous Cell Carcinoma presenting as a leukoplakic plaque on the shaft.

Front: Major risk factor for Penile Squamous Cell Carcinoma?

Back: HPV infection and lack of circumcision (poor hygiene/smegma).

Front: Hallmark of Sertoli-Leydig Cell Tumors?

Back: Re-masculinization or virilization in females.

Front: Pathology of Brenner Tumor?

Back: Ovarian tumor containing "Walnut-like" nuclei and urothelial (bladder-like) epithelium.

Front: Definition of Endometrial Polyp?

Back: Benign exophytic growth of endometrial glands and stroma; often associated with Tamoxifen.

Front: Mechanism of fat necrosis in the breast?

Back: Trauma-induced release of fatty acids leading to saponification and calcification.

Front: Most common location for an ectopic pregnancy?

Back: Ampulla of the Fallopian tube.

Front: Definition of Asherman Syndrome?

Back: Intrauterine adhesions (synechiae) following over-aggressive D&C, leading to infertility.

Front: Cause of Vulvar Vestibulitis?

Back: Inflammation of small glands in the vestibule causing severe entry dyspareunia.

Front: Significance of a high mitotic count in a smooth muscle tumor of the uterus?

Back: Suggests Leiomyosarcoma rather than Leiomyoma.

Front: Common site of origin for Serous Tubal Intraepithelial Carcinoma (STIC)?

Back: Fimbria of the Fallopian tube.

Front: Histology of Comedocarcinoma (DCIS subtype)?

Back: Central necrosis with calcifications within the duct.

Front: Preferred staging system for Gynecologic cancers?

Back: FIGO Staging.

Front: Most significant prognostic factor in Breast Cancer?

Back: Axillary lymph node status.

Front: Effect of Triple Negative Breast Cancer on prognosis?

Back: Poor prognosis (ER-, PR-, HER2-).

Front: Hormone changes in Menopause?

Back: Decreased estrogen, significantly increased FSH (loss of negative feedback).

Front: Typical age for testicular torsion?

Back: Neonates or adolescents (bell-clapper deformity).

Front: Distinction between Seminoma and Non-seminomatous tumors regarding radiosensitivity?

Back: Seminomas are highly radiosensitive.

Front: Typical immunohistochemistry for Dysgerminoma?

Back: OCT3/4, PLAP, and c-KIT (CD117).

Front: Risk factors for Vulvar Squamous Cell Carcinoma (Non-HPV related)?

Back: Long-standing Lichen Sclerosus in elderly women.

Front: Definition of Chronic Prostatitis?

Back: Recurrent UTIs or pelvic pain with/without bacterial growth in prostatic secretions.

Front: Relationship between breast density and cancer risk?

Back: Increased radiographic density correlates with higher risk.

Front: Effect of breastfeeding on breast cancer risk?

Back: Protective (decreases lifetime estrogen exposure).

Front: Most common cause of Acute Mastitis?

Back: Staphylococcus aureus (usually during breastfeeding).

Front: Pathology of Periductal Mastitis?

Back: Subareolar inflammation seen mostly in smokers (Vitamin A deficiency leads to squamous metaplasia).

Front: Gross description of Mucinous (Colloid) Carcinoma of the breast?

Back: "Blue-gray" gelatinous mass.

Front: Precursor lesion to most Ovarian Surface Epithelial tumors?

Back: Inclusion cysts or fimbrial epithelium.

Front: Histologic feature of Clear Cell Adenocarcinoma of the ovary?

Back: Large cells with clear cytoplasm and "hobnail" cells.

Front: Definition of Endolymphatic Stromal Myosis?

Back: Low-grade endometrial stromal sarcoma that infiltrates vessels.

Front: Definition of Decidualization?

Back: Changes in endometrial stroma in response to progesterone to prepare for implantation.

Front: Distinction between Squamous Cell Carcinoma and Adenocarcinoma of the cervix?

Back: Both caused by HPV, but SCC arises from the exocervix and Adeno from the endocervical glands.

Front: Common cause of spontaneous abortion in the first trimester?

Back: Chromosomal abnormalities (e.g., aneuploidy).

Front: Definition of Placenta Accreta?

Back: Placental attachment directly to the myometrium due to a defective decidua.

Front: Difference between Placenta Increta and Percreta?

Back: Increta invades myometrium; Percreta invades through to the serosa/bladder.

Front: Leading cause of Maternal Death in the developed world?

Back: Thromboembolism or Preeclampsia complications.

Front: Histologic marker for Choriocarcinoma?

Back: Proliferation of cytotrophoblasts and syncytiotrophoblasts without villi.

Front: Mechanism of Hemolytic Disease of the Newborn?

Back: Maternal IgG antibodies cross the placenta and attack fetal RBCs (Rh incompatibility).

Front: Definition of Hydrops Fetalis?

Back: Severe fetal edema caused by anemia (immune or non-immune).

Front: Most common benign neoplasm of the fallopian tube?

Back: Adenomatoid tumor.

Front: Clinical sign of Hydrocele?

Back: Transillumination of the scrotum.

Front: Presentation of Testicular Lymphoma?

Back: Most common testicular mass in men >60; usually Diffuse Large B-Cell Lymphoma.

Front: Target of Trastuzumab (Herceptin) in breast cancer?

Back: HER2/neu (ERBB2) receptor.

Front: Precursor to Invasive Lobular Carcinoma?

Back: Lobular Carcinoma In Situ (LCIS).

Front: Management of LCIS?

Back: Observation or Tamoxifen (considered a risk factor rather than a direct precursor).

Front: Hallmark of Paget's Disease of the Vulva?

Back: Intraepidermal spread of malignant cells; usually no underlying invasive cancer (unlike breast).

Front: Most common cause of vulvar "strawberry spots" on the cervix?

Back: Trichomonas vaginalis infection.

Front: Characteristic feature of Lymphogranuloma Venereum (LGV) in the chronic phase?

Back: Fibrosis and "esthiomene" (vulvar elephantiasis).

Front: Primary histologic feature of Granuloma Inguinale (Donovanosis)?

Back: Donovan bodies (intracellular safety-pin shaped bacteria) in macrophages.

Front: Type of epithelium replaced in Vaginal Adenosis?

Back: Squamous epithelium is replaced by columnar (glandular) epithelium.

Front: Most common site for Vulvar Melanoma?

Back: Labia minora and clitoris.

Front: Defining histologic feature of Hidradenoma Papilliferum?

Back: Complex papillary structures lined by a double layer of cells (myoepithelial and secretory).

Front: Primary risk factor for Malignant Mixed Müllerian Tumor (MMMT)?

Back: Prior pelvic radiation therapy.

Uterus: Endometrium & Myometrium

Front: Effect of Tamoxifen on the uterus vs. the breast?

Back: Antagonist in the breast, but Pro-estrogenic (agonist) in the endometrium.

Front: Characteristic finding in Chronic Endometritis biopsy?

Back: Presence of Plasma Cells (lymphocytes alone are insufficient for diagnosis).

Front: Common clinical consequence of an Inadequate Luteal Phase?

Back: Infertility or repeated early pregnancy loss due to low progesterone.

Front: Definition of Endometrial Metaplasia?

Back: Replacement of normal endometrial glands with squamous or tubal cells.

Front: Histologic feature of Endometrial Stromal Sarcoma?

Back: Proliferation of cells resembling stromal cells during the proliferative phase.

Front: Standard treatment for Endometrial Hyperplasia with Atypia?

Back: Hysterectomy (due to high risk of progression to carcinoma).

Front: Effect of oral contraceptive pills (OCPs) on Endometrial Cancer risk?

Back: Protective (progestin counteracts estrogen).

Ovarian Tumors (The "Small Print" Robbins Topics)

Front: Classification of a Brenner Tumor?

Back: Surface epithelial tumor with urothelial (bladder-like) differentiation.

Front: Ovarian tumor associated with Pseudomyxoma Peritonei?

Back: Mucinous Cystadenocarcinoma (often involving the appendix).

Front: Primary difference between Serous and Mucinous ovarian tumors?

Back: Serous are more often bilateral; Mucinous are more often unilateral and larger.

Front: Defining feature of "Chocolate Cyst"?

Back: Endometrioma (endometriosis of the ovary filled with old blood).

Front: Tumor marker for Sertoli-Leydig Cell Tumors?

Back: Testosterone (and potentially Inhibin).

Front: Characteristic of Ovarian Fibroma Grossly?

Back: Solid, firm, white, and whorled (resembles a uterine leiomyoma).

Front: What is a Walthard Cell Rest?

Back: Small nests of transitional-like cells on the fallopian tube/ovary surface (benign).

Front: Most common primary cancer that metastasizes to the ovary?

Back: Gastric Carcinoma (Krukenberg) or Colon Carcinoma.

Gestational Pathology (Molecular & Rare)

Front: Presence of fetal tissue in a Partial vs. Complete Mole?

Back: Partial Mole contains fetal tissue; Complete Mole does not.

Front: Risk of invasive mole or choriocarcinoma after a Complete Mole?

Back: Approximately 15% to 20%.

Front: Defining vascular change in Preeclampsia?

Back: Fibrinoid necrosis and atherosis of the spiral arteries in the placenta.

Front: Definition of Amniotic Fluid Embolism?

Back: Entry of amniotic fluid into maternal circulation causing DIC and respiratory failure.

Front: Histologic finding in Amniotic Fluid Embolism?

Back: Squamous cells and lanugo hair in the maternal pulmonary microvasculature.

Front: What is a Velamentous Cord Insertion?

Back: Umbilical cord inserts into the fetal membranes rather than the placental mass.

Front: Definition of Vasa Previa?

Back: Fetal vessels crossing the internal os of the cervix (high risk of fetal exsanguination).

Male Reproductive: Prostate & Penis

Front: Gleason Score calculation basis?

Back: Sum of the primary (most common) and secondary (second most common) architectural patterns.

Front: Typical Gleason Score indicating high-grade, aggressive cancer?

Back: 8 to 10 (Gleason Pattern 4 and 5).

Front: Histologic marker absent in Prostatic Adenocarcinoma?

Back: Basal cell layer (absent in malignant glands).

Front: Effect of 5-alpha reductase inhibitors on BPH?

Back: Blocks conversion of Testosterone to DHT, shrinking the prostate volume.

Front: Term for "Hardening of the Penis" due to fibrous plaque in the tunica albuginea?Back: Peyronie Disease.  

Front: Define Erythroplasia of Queyrat?

Back: Squamous cell carcinoma in situ (CIS) of the glans penis, presenting as a red plaque.

Front: Clinical presentation of Bowenoid Papulosis?

Back: Multiple reddish-brown papules on the penis; associated with HPV 16; rarely progresses to cancer.

Male Reproductive: Testis & Scrotum

Front: Serum marker often elevated in Seminomas?

Back: Placental Alkaline Phosphatase (PLAP).

Front: Is a Seminoma usually painful or painless?

Back: Painless enlargement of the testis.

Front: Tumor marker elevated in 100% of Choriocarcinomas?

Back: hCG (Human Chorionic Gonadotropin).

Front: Most common component of Mixed Germ Cell Tumors?

Back: Embryonal Carcinoma.

Front: Distinction between Embryonal Carcinoma and Seminoma?

Back: Embryonal is more aggressive, often has hemorrhage/necrosis, and forms primitive glands.

Front: Most common cause of Orchitis in a young adult?

Back: Chlamydia or Gonorrhea.

Front: Most common cause of Orchitis in an older man (>35)?

Back: E. coli or Pseudomonas (UTI-related).

Front: Unique complication of Mumps in post-pubertal males?

Back: Orchitis (can lead to infertility, but usually unilateral).

Breast Pathology: Molecular & Subtypes

Front: Significance of "Radial Scar" on mammography?

Back: Mimics carcinoma but is a benign lesion with a fibroelastotic center.

Front: Defining feature of Sclerosing Adenosis?

Back: Increased number of acini with stromal fibrosis and calcification.

Front: Distinction between DCIS and LCIS regarding bilaterality?

Back: LCIS is much more likely to be bilateral and multifocal.

Front: Effect of HER2/neu amplification on breast cancer treatment?

Back: Predicts response to Trastuzumab (anti-HER2 antibody).

Front: Molecular marker used to predict benefit of chemotherapy in ER+ breast cancer?

Back: Oncotype DX (multigene assay).

Front: Triple Negative Breast Cancer is most common in which population?

Back: African American women and BRCA1 mutation carriers.

Front: Characteristics of Lobular Carcinoma In Situ (LCIS) cells?

Back: Small, dyscohesive cells due to lack of E-cadherin.

Front: Most common cause of Galactorrhea in non-pregnant women?

Back: Prolactinoma (Pituitary tumor).

Syndromes & Genetic Correlations

Front: Genetic syndrome associated with bilateral ovarian fibromas?

Back: Gorlin Syndrome (Nevoid Basal Cell Carcinoma Syndrome).

Front: Association of Granulosa Cell Tumors with the endometrium?

Back: Secreted estrogen causes Endometrial Hyperplasia or Carcinoma.

Front: Mutation found in most Leydig Cell Tumors?

Back: GNAS mutations.

Front: Common karyotype of Klinefelter Syndrome?

Back: 47,XXY.

Front: Why does Klinefelter Syndrome cause high FSH?Back: Seminiferous tubule dysgenesis leads to decreased Inhibin B.  

Front: Primary defect in Androgen Insensitivity Syndrome?

Back: Defective Androgen Receptor (X-linked).

Front: Why do AIS patients have high LH?

Back: Resistance to androgen feedback at the hypothalamus/pituitary.

Front: Definition of Swyer Syndrome?

Back: 46,XY karyotype with a SRY mutation resulting in a phenotypic female with streak gonads.

Diagnostics & Miscellaneous

Front: Best stain to visualize basement membrane in the vagina?

Back: Periodic Acid-Schiff (PAS).

Front: Use of Inhibin as a tumor marker?

Back: Monitoring Granulosa Cell Tumors.

Front: Diagnostic significance of a "Bag of Worms" on physical exam?

Back: Varicocele.

Front: Expected hCG level in a patient with an Ectopic Pregnancy?

Back: Lower than expected for gestational age (fails to double every 48 hours).

Front: Histologic feature of Condyloma Acuminatum?Back: Koilocytic atypia (wrinkled nuclei with perinuclear halos).  

Front: Term for fusion of labia due to low estrogen in children?

Back: Labial Adhesions.

Front: Primary cause of "Inadequate Luteal Phase" in older women?

Back: Diminished ovarian reserve (failing corpus luteum).

Front: Common cause of hematocolpos (blood in the vagina)?

Back: Imperforate Hymen.

Front: Defining feature of Turner Syndrome on the ovary?

Back: Streak ovaries (accelerated oocyte loss).

Front: Most common location for an accessory nipple (Polythelia)?

Back: Along the milk line (axilla to groin).

Front: Characteristic histologic feature of a Fibroepithelial Polyp (Skin Tag)?

Back: Fibrovascular core covered by benign squamous epithelium.

Front: Most common benign tumor of the vulva?

Back: Condyloma Acuminatum (Genital Warts).

Front: Definition of Vulvar Intraepithelial Neoplasia (VIN)?

Back: Dysplastic squamous cells within the vulvar epidermis, graded I-III.

Front: Primary treatment for high-grade VIN?

Back: Surgical excision or laser ablation.

Front: Relationship between Lichen Sclerosus and Squamous Cell Carcinoma?

Back: Chronic inflammation leads to p53 mutations in the absence of HPV.

Uterine Corpus & Tubes

Front: Histologic appearance of Endometrial Stromal Nodule?

Back: Well-circumscribed proliferation of endometrial stromal cells with no vascular invasion.

Front: Distinction between Low-grade and High-grade Endometrial Stromal Sarcoma?

Back: Low-grade has minimal atypia and infiltrates vessels; High-grade is pleomorphic and aggressive.

Front: Primary cause of Salpingitis?

Back: Ascending infection from the lower genital tract (Gonorrhea/Chlamydia).

Front: Definition of Hydrosalpinx?

Back: Distention of the fallopian tube with clear fluid, often following chronic salpingitis.

Front: Most common benign tumor of the Fallopian Tube?

Back: Adenomatoid Tumor (mesothelial origin).

Ovarian: Specialized & Stromal

Front: Characteristic clinical feature of a Hilus Cell Tumor?

Back: Virilization due to testosterone production; contains Reinke crystals.

Front: Defining feature of Gonadoblastoma?

Back: Rare tumor found in dysgenetic gonads (e.g., Swyer Syndrome) containing germ cells and sex cord-stroma.

Front: Most common source of Pseudomyxoma Peritonei?

Back: Appendiceal mucinous neoplasm (rarely primary ovarian).

Front: Pathogenesis of Ovarian Torsion?

Back: Twisting of the ovary on its suspensory ligament, compromising blood flow.

Front: "Bell-clapper deformity" is to the testis as ______ is to the ovary.

Back: An enlarged ovarian mass (dermoid or cyst).

Male Reproductive: Specialized Pathology

Front: Definition of Phimosis?

Back: Inability to retract the prepuce (foreskin) over the glans penis.

Front: Definition of Paraphimosis?

Back: Prepuce is trapped behind the glans, causing vascular compromise (medical emergency).

Front: Typical presentation of Balanoposthitis?

Back: Inflammation of the glans and prepuce, commonly in uncircumcised men with poor hygiene.

Front: Most common type of Penile Cancer?

Back: Squamous Cell Carcinoma.

Front: Distinguishing feature of a Spermatocele?

Back: Cystic accumulation of sperm in the head of the epididymis.

Front: Histology of a Leydig Cell Tumor?

Back: Eosinophilic cytoplasm with characteristic Reinke crystals.

Front: Hormonal profile in a Sertoli Cell Tumor?

Back: Usually hormonally silent, but can rarely produce estrogens.

Breast: Rare & High-Yield

Front: Characteristic of Diabetic Mastopathy?

Back: Firm, painless breast mass in Type 1 Diabetics showing lymphocytic lobulitis and fibrosis.

Front: Histology of Granulomatous Mastitis?

Back: Non-caseating granulomas involving the lobules, often idiopathic or associated with Sarcoidosis.

Front: Most common cause of a "Palpable Mass" in a woman under 30?

Back: Fibroadenoma.

Front: Definition of Atypical Ductal Hyperplasia (ADH)?

Back: Proliferation of monomorphic cells partially filling a duct; increases breast cancer risk by 4-5x.

Front: Common mutation in Familial Breast Cancer (other than BRCA)?

Back: TP53 (Li-Fraumeni Syndrome) or PTEN (Cowden Syndrome).

Front: Characteristic appearance of "Comedo" DCIS on gross pathology?

Back: Toothpaste-like necrotic material squeezed from the ducts.

Pregnancy & Placenta (Molecular)

Front: Pathophysiology of HELLP Syndrome?

Back: Severe preeclampsia leading to Hemolysis, Elevated Liver enzymes, and Low Platelets.

Front: Definition of Chorioamnionitis?

Back: Bacterial infection of the fetal membranes, usually ascending from the vagina.

Front: Gross appearance of the placenta in Chorioamnionitis?

Back: Opaque, friable, and foul-smelling membranes.

Front: Result of a "Circumvallate Placenta"?

Back: A double layer of chorion and amnion creates a ring, increasing risk of premature rupture.

Front: Primary cause of Abruptio Placentae?

Back: Maternal hypertension, cocaine use, or trauma.

Systemic/Syndromic Associations

Front: Association between Peutz-Jeghers Syndrome and the reproductive system?

Back: Increased risk of Sex Cord Tumors with Annular Tubules (SCTAT).

Front: Ovarian tumor associated with Gorlin Syndrome?

Back: Ovarian Fibroma.

Front: Reproductive association with von Hippel-Lindau (VHL) disease?

Back: Epididymal cystadenomas.

Front: Genetic basis of Congenital Adrenal Hyperplasia (most common type)?

Back: 21-hydroxylase deficiency (leads to virilization in females).

Front: Definition of Kallmann Syndrome?

Back: Hypogonadotropic hypogonadism and anosmia (failure of GnRH neuron migration).

Front: Relationship between Myasthenia Gravis and reproductive pathology?

Back: Associated with Thymoma, which can be confused with germ cell tumors in the mediastinum.

Front: Effect of Mumps on fertility?

Back: Can cause permanent sterility if orchitis is bilateral and severe.

Front: Most common site of extra-nodal Lymphoma in the male reproductive system?

Back: The testis.

Front: Most common oncogenic driver in Serous Cystadenocarcinoma (High Grade)?

Back: TP53 mutation (present in >95% of cases).

Front: Genetic mutation pathognomonic for Low-Grade Serous Ovarian Carcinoma?

Back: KRAS or BRAF mutations.

Front: Primary molecular defect in McCune-Albright Syndrome affecting the ovary?

Back: GNAS gene mutation leading to constitutive activation of FSH receptors and precocious puberty.

Front: FOXL2 mutation is a diagnostic marker for which tumor?

Back: Adult-type Granulosa Cell Tumor.

Front: DICER1 mutations are commonly associated with which pediatric ovarian tumor?

Back: Sertoli-Leydig Cell Tumor.

Front: The "Two-Hit Hypothesis" in BRCA1/2 related breast cancer refers to?

Back: An inherited germline mutation followed by a somatic loss of the remaining wild-type allele.

Specific Histological Variants

Front: Classic histologic feature of "Hobnail Cells" is seen in?

Back: Clear Cell Carcinoma of the ovary or endometrium.

Front: Defining histological feature of an Adenomatoid Tumor?

Back: Vacuolated mesothelial cells forming gland-like spaces (often in the epididymis or fallopian tube).

Front: Presence of "Call-Exner-like" spaces in a male testicular tumor suggests?

Back: Sertoli Cell Tumor.

Front: Hallmark of Microcystic Adnexal Carcinoma of the vulva?

Back: Deeply invasive nests of small cysts and cords with perineural invasion.

Front: Definition of "Starry Sky" appearance in the reproductive system?

Back: Burkitt Lymphoma (can involve the ovaries or testes).

Advanced Male Reproductive Pathology

Front: Term for the precursor lesion of most Testicular Germ Cell Tumors?

Back: Germ Cell Neoplasia In Situ (GCNIS).

Front: Which testicular germ cell tumor does NOT arise from GCNIS?

Back: Pediatric Yolk Sac Tumor and Teratoma.

Front: Primary immunohistochemical marker for Seminoma?

Back: OCT3/4 and SALL4.

Front: Histologic appearance of Spermatocytic Tumor (formerly Spermatocytic Seminoma)?

Back: Three cell populations (small, medium, and giant) with "spireme" chromatin.

Front: Typical behavior of a Spermatocytic Tumor vs. a Classic Seminoma?

Back: Spermatocytic Tumors occur in older men and rarely metastasize.

Front: Clinical significance of "High-Grade Prostatic Intraepithelial Neoplasia" (HGPIN)?

Back: Precursor to prostatic adenocarcinoma; requires close follow-up but not immediate surgery.

Clinical Correlation & "Pearls"

Front: What is the "Schiller-Duval body" analogous to in embryology?

Back: A primitive glomerulus.

Front: Definition of "Pseudopityriasis Rosea" in the context of Syphilis?

Back: The generalized maculopapular rash of secondary syphilis involving the palms and soles.

Front: Cause of "Violin-string" adhesions in Fitz-Hugh-Curtis Syndrome?

Back: Chronic inflammation and scarring of the liver capsule following PID.

Front: Most common cause of a "Cold" (non-functional) nodule in the testis?

Back: Any solid testicular mass should be considered malignant until proven otherwise (orchiectomy).

Front: Significance of a "Blue Dot Sign" on the scrotum?

Back: Torsion of the appendix testis (benign, non-surgical).

Front: What is the "Whiff Test" used for?

Back: Diagnosis of Bacterial Vaginosis (addition of KOH produces a fishy odor).

Breast: High-Level Concepts

Front: Definition of "Flat Epithelial Atypia" (FEA) in the breast?

Back: Replacement of native terminal duct lobular unit cells by one or more layers of atypical cuboidal/columnar cells.

Front: Most common cause of "Granulomatous Lobular Mastitis"?

Back: Possible autoimmune reaction or reaction to Corynebacterium (seen in parous women).

Front: Defining feature of "Inflammatory Breast Cancer" on biopsy?

Back: Dermal lymphatic invasion by tumor emboli.

Front: Histologic distinction of Medullary Carcinoma of the breast?

Back: Pushing borders, syncytial growth pattern, and prominent lymphoplasmacytic infiltrate.

Front: Significance of the "Phyllodes" leaf-like architecture?

Back: Overgrowth of the stromal component relative to the epithelial component.

Placental & Developmental Nuances

Front: Primary risk associated with a "Bipartite Placenta" (Placenta Bilobata)?

Back: Increased risk of vasa previa and postpartum hemorrhage.

Front: Definition of "Amnion Nodosum"?

Back: Small, yellow-white nodules on the fetal surface of the amnion; associated with severe oligohydramnios.

Front: Most common cause of "Single Umbilical Artery" (SUA)?

Back: Usually idiopathic, but associated with an increased risk of fetal chromosomal and renal anomalies.

Front: Pathogenesis of "Gestational Alloimmune Thrombocytopenia"?

Back: Maternal antibodies (anti-HPA-1a) cross the placenta and destroy fetal platelets.

Front: Characteristic finding in "Cervical Intraepithelial Neoplasia" (CIN) Grade III?

Back: Full-thickness loss of maturation and high nuclear-to-cytoplasmic ratio.


Front: IHC marker to differentiate Sex Cord-Stromal tumors from Epithelial tumors?
Back: Inhibin (Positive in Sex Cord-Stromal).

Front: Characteristic IHC profile for Embryonal Carcinoma?
Back: CD30 positive, CK positive, and OCT3/4 positive.

Front: IHC stain used to confirm a diagnosis of Mesothelioma in the tunica vaginalis?
Back: Calretinin.

Front: Use of p16 IHC in cervical pathology?
Back: Strong, diffuse staining indicates high-risk HPV (E7) activity.

Front: IHC marker for Clear Cell Carcinoma of the ovary?
Back: HNF-1-beta.

Clinical Syndromes & Drug Effects
Front: Term for "Ovarian hyperstimulation" causing massive cystic enlargement?
Back: Theca Lutein Cysts (associated with high hCG or fertility drugs).

Front: Classic drug cause of Endometrial Polyps?
Back: Tamoxifen.

Front: Reproductive side effect of Spironolactone in males?
Back: Gynecomastia (due to its anti-androgenic effects).

Front: Condition of the breast associated with long-term Cimetidine use?
Back: Gynecomastia.

Front: Definition of "Stein-Leventhal Syndrome"?
Back: The original name for Polycystic Ovarian Syndrome (PCOS).

Rare & Complex Variants
Front: Definition of "Large Cell Calcifying Sertoli Cell Tumor"?
Back: A rare Sertoli cell tumor associated with Carney Complex.

Front: Defining feature of "Serous Surface Papillary Carcinoma"?
Back: Primary peritoneal cancer that is histologically identical to ovarian serous carcinoma but the ovaries are minimally involved.

Front: Histologic hallmark of "Microglandular Hyperplasia" of the cervix?
Back: Proliferation of small glands, often associated with pregnancy or OCP use; mimics adenocarcinoma.

Front: What is a "Decidual Cast"?
Back: The entire thickened endometrial lining shed in one piece, often seen in ectopic pregnancy or sudden progesterone drop.

Front: Definition of "Placental Site Trophoblastic Tumor" (PSTT)?
Back: Rare tumor of intermediate trophoblasts; secretes Human Placental Lactogen (hPL) rather than hCG.

Specific Developmental Anomalies
Front: Definition of "Gartner Duct Cyst"?
Back: Remnant of the Wolffian (Mesonephric) duct on the lateral wall of the vagina.

Front: Definition of "Müllerian Agenesis" (Mayer-Rokitansky-Küster-Hauser syndrome)?
Back: Congenital absence of the uterus and upper 2/3 of the vagina in 46,XX females.

Front: Result of a "Bicornuate Uterus"?
Back: Incomplete fusion of the Müllerian ducts, resulting in a heart-shaped uterus with two horns.

Front: Origin of a "Paraovarian Cyst"?
Back: Remnants of the Wolffian duct located in the broad ligament (e.g., Hydatid of Morgagni).

Vascular & Inflammatory Extras
Front: Most common cause of "Granulomatous Orchitis"?
Back: Idiopathic (suspected autoimmune) or Tuberculosis.

Front: Pathogenesis of "Fournier Gangrene"?
Back: Necrotizing fasciitis of the perineum/scrotum; a surgical emergency.

Front: "Yellow-brown" pigment in a "Luteoma of Pregnancy"?
Back: Intracellular lipid.

Front: Clinical behavior of a "Luteoma of Pregnancy"?
Back: Benign, regresses spontaneously postpartum, but can cause maternal virilization.

Front: Definition of "Squamous Metaplasia of Lactiferous Ducts" (SMULD)?
Back: Periductal mastitis; keratin plugs the duct leading to abscess and fistula formation in smokers.

Final High-Yield Odds & Ends
Front: Primary risk factor for Primary Vaginal Melanoma?
Back: Unlike skin melanoma, it is not UV-related; etiology is unknown.

Front: Key difference between "Low-grade" and "High-grade" SIL (Squamous Intraepithelial Lesion)?
Back: LSIL typically regresses (HPV 6, 11); HSIL has a high risk of progression (HPV 16, 18).

Front: Primary symptom of "Torsion of the Appendix Testis"?
Back: Sudden onset localized pain with a palpable "blue dot" on the upper scrotum.

Front: Definition of "Prehn Sign"?
Back: Relief of scrotal pain with elevation (Positive in epididymitis, Negative in torsion).

Front: Most common cause of "Azoospermia" (no sperm) in cystic fibrosis?
Back: Congenital Bilateral Absence of the Vas Deferens (CBAVD).

Front: How to distinguish a Seminoma from a Dysgerminoma?
Back: They are histologically identical; the name depends on the gender of the patient (Seminoma in males, Dysgerminoma in females).

Front: Distinguishing feature of Embryonal Carcinoma vs. Yolk Sac Tumor markers?
Back: Embryonal is usually AFP negative (or low), while Yolk Sac is always AFP positive.

Front: Histologic hallmark of a Granular Cell Tumor of the vulva?
Back: S100 positive cells with abundant eosinophilic granules and pseudoepitheliomatous hyperplasia (mimics SCC).

Front: Primary difference between Follicular Cysts and Theca Lutein Cysts?
Back: Follicular cysts are usually solitary; Theca Lutein cysts are multiple, bilateral, and driven by high hCG.

Front: Key histologic distinction between Paget’s Disease of the Breast and Melanoma?
Back: Paget cells contain mucin (PAS positive) and are S100 negative.

Specific Molecular Pathology & IHC (Level 2)
Front: Which breast cancer subtype is most associated with the "Basal-like" gene expression?
Back: Triple-negative breast cancer (ER-, PR-, HER2-).

Front: Primary molecular driver in Phyllodes Tumors?
Back: MED12 mutations.

Front: Molecular hallmark of Alveolar Rhabdomyosarcoma (Sarcoma Botryoides variant)?
Back: t(2;13) or t(1;13) translocation involving PAX3 or PAX7.

Front: IHC marker used to distinguish between a Complete and Partial Mole?
Back: p57 (expressed in Partial Moles; absent in Complete Moles due to lack of maternal DNA).

Front: Genetic mutation associated with Sertoli-Leydig tumors in Pleuropulmonary Blastoma?
Back: DICER1 mutation.

Placental & Umbilical Cord Micro-Pathology
Front: Definition of Placental Infarction?
Back: Localized necrosis of villi due to compromised maternal blood flow (common in Preeclampsia).

Front: Histologic sign of a CMV infection in the placenta?
Back: "Owl’s eye" intranuclear inclusions in enlarged villous cells.

Front: Definition of Chorangioma?
Back: A benign, hamartomatous capillary hemangioma of the placenta.

Front: Significance of "Hoffbauer Cells" in the placenta?
Back: They are fetal macrophages found in the villous stroma.

Front: Consequence of "True Knots" in the umbilical cord?
Back: Can lead to fetal hypoxia and intrauterine fetal death (IUFD).

Clinical Signs & Eponyms
Front: What is "Schwartzman Reaction" in the context of pregnancy?
Back: A form of DIC and renal cortical necrosis triggered by endotoxins (often associated with septic abortion).

Front: Definition of "Arias-Stella Reaction"?
Back: Hypersecretory endometrial changes (enlarged nuclei) seen in pregnancy or ectopic pregnancy; mimics malignancy.

Front: Classic presentation of "Peyronie Disease"?
Back: Fibrous plaque in the tunica albuginea causing painful curvature of the penis during erection.

Front: Definition of "Kraurosis Vulvae"?
Back: Severe atrophy and shrinkage of the vulva, often the end-stage of Lichen Sclerosus.

Front: Cause of "Pseudohermaphroditism" in males?
Back: Most commonly Androgen Insensitivity Syndrome (46,XY with female phenotype).

Male Reproductive: Detailed Anatomy & Rare Tumors
Front: Primary histologic feature of "Intratubular Germ Cell Neoplasia"?
Back: Atypical germ cells limited to the seminiferous tubules (precursor to most GCTs).

Front: Most common site for a "Sperm Granuloma"?
Back: The epididymis (often follows a vasectomy).

Front: Definition of "Tunica Vaginalis Hydrocele"?
Back: Fluid collection between the parietal and visceral layers of the tunica vaginalis.

Front: Mechanism of "Testicular Infarction" in Torsion?
Back: Venous obstruction occurs first, followed by arterial compromise and hemorrhagic necrosis.

Front: Histology of "Condyloma Acuminatum" vs. "Condyloma Latum"?
Back: Acuminatum shows koilocytic atypia (HPV); Latum shows plasma cell-rich inflammation (Syphilis).

Advanced Breast: Rare Findings
Front: Definition of "Cystosarcoma Phyllodes"?
Back: An older term for Phyllodes Tumor, emphasizing its "leaf-like" and potentially malignant nature.

Front: Distinguishing feature of "Secretory Carcinoma" of the breast?
Back: Rare, low-grade cancer that produces abundant intracellular and extracellular secretions; seen in children (Juvenile Carcinoma).

Front: Common location for "Ectopic Breast Tissue"?
Back: The axilla (most common site along the milk line).

Front: Definition of "Mondor’s Disease"?
Back: Superficial thrombophlebitis of the breast veins; presents as a painful, cord-like mass.

Front: Significance of "Atypical Lobular Hyperplasia" (ALH)?
Back: Similar to LCIS but cells fill <50% of the acini in a lobule; indicates increased cancer risk.

Final Synthesis: USMLE "Must-Knows"
Front: Most common cause of "Scrotal Pain" in a 20-year-old?
Back: Epididymitis (usually Chlamydia or Gonorrhea).

Front: Most common cause of "Scrotal Pain" in a 12-year-old?
Back: Testicular Torsion.

Front: Definition of "Hematocle"?
Back: Blood in the tunica vaginalis, usually following trauma.

Front: Result of "Chronic Endometritis" on fertility?
Back: Increases risk of ectopic pregnancy and infertility due to tubal scarring.

Front: Hallmark of "Postpartum Pituitary Necrosis" (Sheehan Syndrome)?
Back: Failure to lactate and loss of pubic hair due to ischemic necrosis of the anterior pituitary.
